SUBJECT: 
Consideration of Bid #S-4-24 for an Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and 
Built Right LLC for the Cheyenne Kiwanis Community House Roof Replacement 
project 
 ______________________________________________ 
Bid #S-4-24 was solicited for the Cheyenne Kiwanis Community House Roof Replacement. 
 
One (1) bidder responded to this bid, and the bid tabulation is attached.  The Bid was received 
from: 
1. Built Right, LLC (Rapid City, SD) 
 
The bid was reviewed by Steve Gaer (Facilities), Vicki Nemecek (Public Works), and Plan One 
Architects (Re-Roof Architect), and their recommendation is to accept the lowest responsive bid 
from Built Right, LLC. 
 
The total Bid Award amount is $179,000.00

CITY OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING 
AGREEMENT 
 
Bid No. S-4-24 
 
Contract No. _______ 
 
THIS AGREEMENT, entered into this ____ day of ___________, 2023, by and between 
the CITY OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING, hereinafter referred to as the “CITY”, and BUILT 
RIGHT, LLC., hereinafter referred to as the “CONTRACTOR”. 
 
WITNESSETH that the Contractor and the City, for the considerations stated herein, 
mutually agree as follows: 
 
ARTICLE 1.  STATEMENT OF WORK.  The Contractor shall furnish all supervision, 
technical personnel, labor, materials, machinery, tools, equipment, and services, including utility 
and transportation services, and perform and complete all work in an efficient and workmanlike 
manner in the construction of the “Cheyenne Kiwanis Community House Roof Replacement” 
project all in strict accordance with the Contract Documents including all addenda thereto, 
numbered and dated: Addendum One (1), dated September 25, 2023. 
 
ARTICLE 2.  RESPONSIBLE DESIGNEE FOR THE CITY.  The Contractor shall in any 
and all matters relating to the scope of services to be provided under this Contract or any other 
provisions herein, contact the City Engineer, or his/her designated representative. 
 
ARTICLE 3.  TIME FRAME FOR COMPLETION.  The services to be performed under 
this Agreement shall commence on the date stipulated in the “Notice to Proceed” that will be issued 
by the City.  The work shall be completed by April 30, 2024.  If the work has not been completed 
within the time stipulated above, including any extensions of time issued by the City for excusable 
delays, the Contractor and his/her sureties shall pay the City fixed, agreed liquidated damages, as 
stipulated in the Supplemental Conditions, for each calendar day of delay until the work is 
completed. 
 
ARTICLE 4.  COMPENSATION AND METHOD OF PAYMENT.  The CITY will pay 
the CONTRACTOR for the performance of the Contract in current funds, the sum of One 
Hundred Seventy-Nine Thousand Dollars ($179,000.00).  In the event there are changes in the 
estimated quantities shown on the Bid Proposal, the unit prices multiplied by the actual quantities 
shall govern, and the total contract amount will be adjusted accordingly.  The City agrees to pay 
the above amount for contractual services in the following manner, upon receipt of appropriate 
documentation: 
 
a. 
The Contractor will be paid on a monthly basis for percentage of estimated work completed.  
Submittal will be at least seven (7) business days prior to the payable due date as established 
annually by the City Treasurer’s Office.  The pay request shall be submitted on the Contract 
Payment Request Form and Itemized Pay Request or the AIA Documents G702 and G703.  
The engineer will review the estimate for approval prior to payment. 
11:09 am, Oct 17 2023

Page 2 of 2 
 
b. 
The City will withhold five percent (5%) of the dollar value of the work completed for a 
minimum of forty-one (41) calendar days after Notice of Final Settlement has been published 
in accordance with Wyo. Stat. §16-6-116.  Upon completion of the work under this Contract, 
the Contractor shall submit a Contractor’s Certificate of Completion; the Consent of Surety; 
Final Waivers of Lien from the Contractor, and all Sub-Contractors, Suppliers and 
Materialmen; Affidavit of Release of Liens; Affidavit of Payment; and a current Workers 
Compensation Certificate.  Final payment will not be made until the above documents have 
been received by the City and all items on the Punch List have been completed, and the 
advertising requirements have been met. 
 
ARTICLE 5.  CONTRACT.  The executed Contract Documents shall consist of the 
following: 
 
1. This Agreement;  
2. Addenda; 
3. Invitation for Bids; 
4. Instructions to Bidders; 
5. Signed Bid Proposal; 
6. General Conditions and Insurance; 
 
 
7. Supplemental Conditions; 
8. Part IV Forms & Notices; 
9. Specifications and Special 
Provisions; and  
10. Drawings. 
11. Bid S-4-24, Attachment G, Federal 
Provisions and Certifications
This Agreement, together with other documents enumerated in this Article 5, which said other 
documents are as fully a part of the Contract as if hereto attached or herein repeated, forms the 
Contract between the parties hereto. 
 
IN WITNESS WHEREOF, THAT the governing body of the City of Cheyenne has 
authorized the Mayor as Executive Officer of the City to enter into this Agreement, and that the 
parties hereto have caused this Agreement to be executed on the day and year in the first part 
herein written. 
ATTEST: 
 
 
 
 
 
CITY OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING

Patrick Collins, Mayor

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2023-10-03_TotalExtraction_57204877.pdf
MEMORANDUM 
TO: 
 
Mayor Patrick Collins, and City Council 
FROM: 
 
TJ Barttelbort, Purchasing Manager 
DATE: 
 
October 18, 2023 
SUBJECT: 
Consideration to Purchase between the City of Cheyenne, and Advanced Comfort 
Solutions for the purchase and installation of Fire Station Vehicle Exhaust Systems 
______________________________________________ 
This is a Sole Source purchase between the City and Advanced Comfort Solutions, therefore no 
bids were requested. 
This proposed purchase, is for the procurement and installation of Fire Station Vehicle Exhaust 
Systems.  The product specified is manufactured by Nederman. 
The Cheyenne Fire Rescue Department has done extensive research on this type of vehicle exhaust 
system, and it has been selected as the vehicle exhaust ventilation system to be installed in the 
three (3) new fire stations currently under construction.   
In order to ensure that the adaptors/mounting plates for the exhaust pipes are interchangeable 
between all apparatus & all fire stations, the Cheyenne Fire Rescue Department must match any 
other/new installations to the same manufacturer ‘Nederman’. 
Nederman has provided documentation certifying their recommendation for selected installers of 
the product, and for Cheyenne, Wyoming, they have recommended Advanced Comfort Solutions.  
Advanced Comfort Solutions is factory certified for the Installation and Maintenance, of the 
unique vehicle exhaust systems. 
The total requested purchase amount is $372,274.00, which includes installation of the vehicle 
exhaust system at 3 existing Fire Stations, and the Fire Training Facility. 
 
Funding: 
001-21-2113-40-32107 (General Fund / Capital Improvements) 
 
 
$128,000.00 
 
020-26-2612-41-43102 (1% Sales Tax 19-22 / Equipment Over $5,000) 
$244,274.00 
 
Grand Total $372,274.00 

11.6.23
11.13.23
Oct 18 2023, 9:36 am

REQUEST TO PURCHASE OVER $75,000.00 
Consideration to Purchase between the City of Cheyenne, and Advanced Comfort Solutions for 
the purchase and installation of Fire Station Vehicle Exhaust Systems.  This is a sole source 
purchase, therefore no bids were requested.  The total requested amount is $372,274.00 
 
This will be reviewed by the City Council Finance Committee on November 6, 2023 and voted 
on at the November 13, 2023 City Council meeting. 
 
TJ Barttelbort, Purchasing Manager 
City of Cheyenne, Wyoming 
(307) 773-1045 
 
Published in:  Wyoming Tribune-Eagle 
Dates: October 20 & 27, 2023

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2023-09-05_TotalExtraction_85975595.pdf
RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY 
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
Office of City Clerk 
 
September 11, 2023  
 
 
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning 
at 6:00 p.m. in Council Chambers and via electronic conference meeting.  Present were: MAYOR 
– Patrick Collins, COUNCIL MEMBERS – Dr. Michelle Aldrich, Bryan Cook, Ken Esquibel, 
Richard Johnson, Pete Laybourn, Dr. Mark Rinne, Scott Roybal, Tom Segrave and Jeff White.  
Also present: Kris Jones, City Clerk.  The pledge of allegiance was recited. 
 
Consent Agenda.  (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to 
be routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no 
separate discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support 
by two other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered 
in its normal sequence on the agenda.  Upon request by Mr. Cook, and pursuant to support received 
by Mr. Esquibel and Mr. White, item #19 was removed from the Consent Agenda.  Upon request 
by Mr. Laybourn, and pursuant to support received by Mr. White and Mr. Segrave, item #25(f) 
was removed from the Consent Agenda. Mr. Segrave moved to approve remaining items, seconded 
by Mr. Esquibel.  Motion carried. Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. 
 
[CA] 
Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on August 28, 2023.  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried. 
               
Mayor Collins opened a public hearing to receive public comments and input relating to 
the Federally Funded Dry Creek Drainage Master Plan Update.  Public comments were made by: 
Harry LaBonde, Jack Palma, Roberta Coates, Ed Heffern, Jeff Geyer, Laramie County 
Conservation District and Steve Achter.  There being no further comments or input, and purpose 
of the hearing having been met, Mayor Collins closed the public hearing.  
 
ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Creating a new Section 15.50.070, Neglected Structure, 
of Chapter 15.50, Historic Design Guideline, of Title 15, Buildings and Construction, of the 
Municipal Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming.  Reporting for Public Services Committee, 
Mr. Cook advised there was no recommendation from the committee.  Mr. Cook moved to approve 
on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. Johnson.  Dr. Rinne moved to amend paragraph B, 
section 12 to read: Review Committee means a group of three individuals that are nominated by 
the CHPB and approved by the City’s Governing Body.  Their purpose will be to investigate 
petitions filed with the Director alleging that a violation of this Chapter has occurred and to 
recommend to the CHPB if further action is necessary.  Ideally, the members of the Committee 
would have a background in Engineering, Real Estate, the Law or knowledge of Cheyenne’s 
history, seconded by Mr. Johnson.  Motion to amend carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the 
governing body.  Main motion as amended failed due to a tie vote. Voting “yes” – Mr. Esquibel, 
Mr. Johnson, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Roybal and Mr. White.  Voting “no” – Dr. Aldrich, Mayor Collins, 
Mr. Cook, Mr. Laybourn and Mr. Segrave.  Public comment was made by: Milward Simpson, 
Historic Preservation Board.    Comments were made by the following members of the governing 
body: Dr. Aldrich, Mr. Esquibel, Mr. Johnson, Mr. Laybourn, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Segrave and Mr. 
White. 
 

 
ORDINANCE –3rd READING – Submitting to the qualified property owners of real 
property within the boundaries of the Cheyenne Downtown Development Authority District of 
Cheyenne, Wyoming, excluding real property used exclusively for residential purposes, at a 
Special Election on Wednesday, October 18, 2023, the following question: ‘Shall a Special 
Assessment of twenty (20) mills be levied upon the assessed value of real property within the 
boundaries of the Cheyenne Downtown Development Authority District, excluding real property 
used exclusively for residential purposes, for a period of four (4) years pursuant to the provisions 
of Wyo. Stat. § 15-9-217(b) for the purposes of (1) Landscaping or maintenance of public areas; 
(2) Planning or management of development or improvement activities; (3) Promotion of public 
events; and (4) Activities in support of business recruitment and development?’.  Reporting for 
Finance Committee, Mr. White moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Dr. 
Aldrich.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. Comment was made 
by the following member of the governing body: Mr. Laybourn. During Mr. Laybourn’s 
comments, Mayor Collins noted comments weren’t germaine to the subject and were out of order. 
(#4522) 
 
ORDINANCE –3rd READING – Amending the Official Zoning Map of the City of 
Cheyenne changing the zoning classification from Frontier Mall PUD - Planned Unit Development 
(Ordinance No. 3132) to CB - Community Business for land located at Lot 7B, Block 1, Frontier 
Mall).  Mr. Cook moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. Johnson.  Motion 
carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. (#4523) 
 
ORDINANCE –3rd READING – Pursuant to Section 2.2.2 Planned Development - 
Regulating Plan or Development Master Plan and Section 5.6.2 PUD - Planned Unit Development 
District of the Unified Development Code, amending Ordinance No. 4286 by modifying certain 
standards of the adopted district, Planned Unit Development (PUD), for Sweetgrass (located 
southeast of the intersection of College Drive and Avenue C).  Mr. Cook moved to approve on 
third and final reading, seconded by Mr. White. Mr. Cook moved to amend by substitute Exhibit 
A dated September 5, 2023, seconded by Mr. Roybal.  Motion to amend by substitute Exhibit A 
carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body.  Main motion as amended by substitute 
Exhibit A carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body.  Comment was made by 
the following member of the governing body: Mayor Collins. (#4524) 
 
ORDINANCE –2nd READING – Amending Section 5.8.2.h of the Unified Development 
Code (UDC), amending Manufactured Homes on Single Platted Properties or Tracts.  Mr. Cook 
advised there was no recommendation from Public Services Committee.  Mr. Cook moved to 
approve on second reading, seconded by Mr. Roybal. Mr. White moved to postpone to September 
25, 2023, seconded by Dr. Aldrich.  Motion to postpone failed.  Voting “yes” – Dr. Aldrich and 
Mr. White. Voting “no” – Mr. Cook, Mr. Esquibel, Mr. Johnson, Mr. Laybourn, Dr. Rinne, Mr.  
Roybal and Mr. Segrave.  Main motion failed. Voting “yes” – Dr. Aldrich, Mr. Esquibel, Mr. 
Johnson, and Mr. White.  Voting “no” – Mr. Cook, Mr. Laybourn, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Roybal and Mr. 
Segrave. Public comment was made by: Michael White.  Comments were made by the following 
members of the governing body: Dr. Aldrich, Mayor Collins, Mr. Esquibel, Mr. Johnson, Dr. 
Rinne and Mr. Segrave.  
 
ORDINANCE –2nd READING – Amending Section 15.08.031, Building Permit Fees-
Exceptions for Incentivization Program, of Chapter 15.08, International Building Code Adopted, 
of Title 15, Buildings and Construction, of the Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, to create 
incentives for attainable housing and historic preservation.  Mr. Cook moved to approve on second 

reading, seconded by Mr. Johnson.  Mr. Cook moved to amend by substitute dated September 8, 
2023, seconded by Mr. White.  Motion to amend by substitute carried.  Voting “yes” – all members 
of the governing body.  Main motion as amended by substitute carried.  Voting “yes” – all members 
of the governing body. Public comment was made by: Chad Willett, Dan Dorsch, Habitat for 
Humanity of Laramie County and Michael White. Comments were made by the following 
members of the governing body: Dr. Aldrich, Mayor Collins, Mr. Johnson and Dr. Rinne. 
 
 
ORDINANCE –1st READING – Amending the Official Zoning Map of the City of 
Cheyenne changing the zoning classification from MUB Mixed-Use Business Emphasis to P 
Public District for land located at Lot 1, Block 1, Back 40 Subdivision, Third Filing – referred to 
Public Services Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. Cook)   
              
 
ORDINANCE –1st READING – Amending the Official Zoning Map of the City of 
Cheyenne changing the zoning classification from CB Community Business to MUB Mixed-Use 
Business for land located on Lots 3, 4, 5, 6, 7, and 8, Block 1, Hitching Post Plaza, 3rd Filing – 
referred to Public Services Committee.  (Sponsor – Mr. Cook) 
 
 
ORDINANCE –1st READING – Amending certain Sections of Chapter 13.20, Wastewater 
Treatment and Disposal of Title 13, Public Services of the Municipal Code of the City of 
Cheyenne, Wyoming, regarding wastewater treatment and disposal – referred to Public Services 
Committee.  (Sponsor – Dr. Rinne)   
 
[CA] 
RESOLUTION – Authorizing the acceptance of a Federal Emergency Management 
Agency FY 2022 Assistance of Firefighters Grant in the amount of $387,043.63 and authorizing 
the contribution of $38,704.37 from the Fifth and Sixth Penny funds in support of this program, 
for the purpose of purchasing breathable air fill stations (FEMA AFG Grant).  Motion (per Consent 
Agenda) carried. (#6348) 
  
[CA] 
RESOLUTION – Supporting the conversion of a portion of Dutcher Ball Fields located at 
4200 Converse Avenue from perpetual public outdoor recreation use and committing to purchase 
and encumber one additional replacement property to be deed restricted for perpetual public 
outdoor recreation use pursuant to the requirements set forth in the Land and Water Conservation 
Fund Act.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#6349) 
  
 
RESOLUTION – Approving the West Downtown Urban Renewal Plan and West 
Downtown Urban Renewal Project pursuant to Wyoming Statute § 15-9-110.  Mr. Cook moved to 
adopt, seconded by Mr. Roybal.  Motion carried. Voting “yes” – all members of the governing 
body with the exception of Dr. Aldrich, Mr. Laybourn and Mr. Segrave voting “no”.  Comments 
were made by the following members of the governing body: Dr. Aldrich and Mr. Segrave. (#6350) 
  
 
RESOLUTION – To accept and approve the federally funded Dry Creek Drainage Master 
Plan Update submitted by GLM Design Group.  Mr. Cook moved to adopt, seconded by Mr. 
Segrave.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body.  Public comments 
were made by: Michael White and Gene MacDonald, GLM Design Group.  Comment was made

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2023-09-05_TotalExtraction_70644470.pdf
Planning and Development Office 
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282   
(Fax) 307-637-6366 
 
 
 
 
MEMO 
To: 
Honorable Mayor Patrick Collins and Members of the City Council 
From: 
Seth Lloyd 
Subject: Building Fee Exception Expansion Ordinance 
Date: 
August 28, 2023 
 
Affordable Housing Task Force Recommendation:  
The fourth Affordable Housing Task Force recommendation from 2022 was that the City create a fee 
waiver for building permits for projects producing affordable housing. The recommendation was part of 
the Affordable Housing Task Force Work Session and Staff was directed to generally work on all items 
presented. The recommendation is attached. 
 
Background:  
The current City code has a section which gives building permits in the downtown area an exception to 
building permit fees. This code section includes a sunset date and sets up the program as more of a re-
imbursement than a waiver. As opposed to creating a new section of code, Staff proposes fulfilling the 
Affordable Housing Task Force’s recommendation by amending the current section. 
 
The proposed code amendment has the following features: 
• 
The inclusion of a fee exception for building permits for the creation of income restricted 
housing (per the recommendation of the Affordable Housing Task Force) 
• 
The inclusion of a fee exception for building permits for the maintenance of historic housing 
(requested as part of the change to this section of code) 
• 
Defining the limit of fees eligible to be reimbursed for the new exception types 
• 
Increasing the limit of fees eligible to be reimbursed for all exception types  
• 
Extending the sunset of the program generally 
The monetary limits were set based on an approximation based on the original downtown program. 
 
Although the Ordinance is proposing modifying the text of the existing section of code, a redline 
ordinance was deemed less clear than cleanly replacing the section. The redline is also attached for 
reference. 
 
The item for consideration is an amendment to City Code. This is approved by Ordinance. The Governing 
Body review schedule is anticipated as follows: 
• 
Introduction and Referral  
8/28/2023 
• 
Finance Committee 
 
9/5/2023 
• 
Second reading  
 
9/11/2023 
• 
Finance Committee 
 
9/18/2023 
• 
Third and final reading  
9/25/2023 
  
  
  
  
  
  
  
  Planning and Development Department 
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282 (Fax) 307-637-6366

1. Ordinance 
2. Redline of existing code 
3. Affordable Housing Task Force Recommendation #4 

 
ORDINANCE NO.____________ 
 
ENTITLED: "AN ORDINANCE AMENDING SECTION 15.08.031, BUILDING PERMIT 
FEES–EXCEPTIONS 
FOR 
INCENTIVIZATION 
PROGRAM, 
OF 
CHAPTER 15.08, INTERNATIONAL BUILDING CODE ADOPTED, OF 
TITLE 15, BUILDINGS AND CONSTRUCTION, OF THE CODE OF THE 
CITY OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING, TO CREATE INCENTIVES FOR 
ATTAINABLE HOUSING AND HISTORIC PRESERVATION." 
 
BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, 
WYOMING: 
 
Section 1.  That Section 15.08.031, Building permit fees–Exceptions for incentivization 
program, of Chapter 15.08, International Building Code Adopted, of Title 15, Buildings and 
Construction, of the Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, is amended to read: 
 
15.08.031 - Building permit fees–Exceptions for incentivization program. 
 
A. Programs that reduce the total amount of project fees paid have been found to successfully 
incentivize property development. The City of Cheyenne governing body desires to 
encourage and incentivize quality projects of the following varieties: 
 
1. Infill and the redevelopment and reuse of existing buildings and property within the 
downtown core; 
 
2. Residential development that contributes to attainable housing available to the 
workforce; and 
 
3. The rehabilitation of historic structures in ways which maintain historic integrity. 
 
B. The "City of Cheyenne Development Incentivization Program" is hereby established. To 
be eligible for the program, an applicant must satisfy the following conditions: 
 
1. Upon a form to be provided, complete and submit an application to the city planning 
and development department;  
 
2. A building permit application must be received by the city building office prior to June 
30, 2030; and 
 
3. Meet the specific requirements of at least one (1) of the following incentivization 
categories: 
 
a. Be located within the boundaries of the core development incentivization program 
project area identified in Exhibit A (map) attached to Ordinance No. 4241, on file 
2:26 pm, Sep 08 2023

with the office of the city clerk and also available through the planning and 
development department; 
 
b. Provide housing that is income-restricted to eighty percent (80%) of area median 
income (AMI) as defined by the U.S. department of housing and urban 
development (HUD). The income restriction required under this subparagraph shall 
be for a period of not less than fifteen (15) years; or 
 
c. Be a contributing historic structure in a historic district and submit a building permit 
application which only proposes changes consistent with maintaining historic 
integrity. 
 
C. The city shall reimburse the building permit fees as specified under Section 15.08.030 of 
this chapter, as provided under this subsection. Building permits issued the previous fiscal 
year shall not be eligible for reimbursements under this subsection if received more than 
seven (7) days after the close of the fiscal year. No reimbursement of a building permit fee 
shall be issued before the completion of the attendant project. Building permit fees shall be 
reimbursed as follows: 
 
1. Up to five thousand dollars ($5,000.00) per program project located within the 
boundaries of the core development incentivization program project area as provided 
by subparagraph (B)(3)(a) of this section; 
 
2. Up to five thousand dollars ($5,000.00) per program project that creates affordable 
single-family housing in accordance with subparagraph (B)(3)(b) of this section; 
 
3. Up to ten thousand dollars ($10,000.00) per program project that creates affordable 
multi-family housing in accordance with subparagraph (B)(3)(b) of this section; or 
 
4. Up to one five thousand dollars ($51,000.00) per program project that rehabilitates a 
historic structure in accordance with subparagraph (B)(3)(c) of this section. 
 
D. Planning and development department staff shall provide reports on prior fiscal year 
program activity to the governing body not less than annually. The reports shall contain: 
 
1. The total number of projects utilizing the program; 
 
2. The total amount of fees waived; and 
 
3. The total valuation of projects incentivized by the program. 
 
E. The total amount of building permit fees reimbursed under this section shall be capped for 
each fiscal year in accordance with the limitations imposed under this section and as 
determined by the director of the planning and development department. Reimbursements 
shall be limited as follows:  
 

1. Not more than $200,000 for the entire program; 
 
2. Not more than $100,000 for downtown core projects; 
 
3. Not more than $150,000 for affordable housing projects; and 
 
4. Not more than $3520,000 for historic rehabilitation projects.  
 
F. A decision from the director of the planning and development department, or their 
designee, on any matters of interpretation in this section shall be final. 
 
G. This section is repealed effective July 1, 2030. 
 
Section 2.  Sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses and phrases of this ordinance are 
severable. If any phrase, clause, sentence, paragraph or section of this ordinance is declared illegal 
or unconstitutional, such illegality or unconstitutionality shall not affect any of the remaining 
phrases, clauses, sentences, paragraphs and sections.  
 
Section 3.  That this ordinance shall be in full force and effect upon its publication.

BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, 
WYOMING: 
 
Section 1.  That Section 15.08.031, Building permit fees–Exceptions for incentivization 
program, of Chapter 15.08, International Building Code Adopted, of Title 15, Buildings and 
Construction, of the Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, is amended to read: 
 
15.08.031 - Building permit fees–Exceptions for incentivization program. 
 
A. Programs that reduce the total amount of project fees paid have been found to successfully 
incentivize property development. The city City of Cheyenne governing body along with 
the downtown development authority desires to encourage and incentivize quality infill 
projects and the redevelopment and reuse of existing buildings and property within the 
downtown core. Programs that reduce the total amount of project fees paid have been found 
to successfully incentivize property development. of the following varieties: 
 
1. Infill and the redevelopment and reuse of existing buildings and property within the 
downtown core; 
 
2. Residential development that contributes to attainable housing available to the 
workforce; and 
 
3. The rehabilitation of historic structures in ways which maintain historic integrity. 
 
B. A program, to be called the The "City of Cheyenne Core Development Incentivization 
Program," is hereby established. To be eligible for the program, an applicant must satisfy 
the following conditions: 
 
1. To be eligible for the program, an applicant must fulfill the following: 
 
a. 1. Upon a form to be provided, complete and submit an application to the city planning 
and development department;.  
 
b. 2. A building permit application must be received by the city building department 
office prior to June 30, 2025. 2030; and 
 

3. Meet the specific requirements of at least one (1) of the following incentivization 
categories: 
 
a. Be located within the boundaries of the core development incentivization program 
project area identified in Exhibit A (map) attached to Ordinance No. 4241, on file 
with the office of the city clerk and also available through the planning and 
development department; 
 
b. Provide housing that is income-restricted to eighty percent (80%) of area median 
income (AMI) as defined by the U.S. department of housing and urban 
development (HUD). The income restriction required under this subparagraph shall 
be for a period of not less than fifteen (15) years; or 
 
c. Be a contributing historic structure in a historic district and submit a building permit 
application which only proposes changes consistent with maintaining historic 
integrity. 
 
2. Reimbursements for building permits issued the previous fiscal year shall not be 
eligible if received more than seven days after the close of the fiscal year. 
 
C. The city shall waive reimburse the building permit fees, as set forth in specified under 
Section 15.08.030 of this chapter, up to five thousand dollars ($5,000.00) per program 
project located within the boundaries of the core development incentivization program 
project area identified in exhibit A (map) attached to ordinance no. 4241. A map of the 
program project area is also available through the planning and development department. 
as provided under this subsection. Building permits issued the previous fiscal year shall 
not be eligible for reimbursements under this subsection if received more than seven (7) 
days after the close of the fiscal year. No reimbursement of a building permit fee shall be 
issued before the completion of the attendant project. Building permit fees shall be 
reimbursed as follows: 
 
1. Up to five thousand dollars ($5,000.00) per program project located within the 
boundaries of the core development incentivization program project area as provided 
by subparagraph (B)(3)(a) of this section; 
 
2. Up to five thousand dollars ($5,000.00) per program project that creates affordable 
single-family housing in accordance with subparagraph (B)(3)(b) of this section; 
 
3. Up to ten thousand dollars ($10,000.00) per program project that creates affordable 
multi-family housing in accordance with subparagraph (B)(3)(b) of this section; or 
 
4. Up to one thousand dollars ($1,000.00) per program project that rehabilitates a historic 
structure in accordance with subparagraph (B)(3)(c) of this section. 
 
D. A decision from the director of planning and development, or their designee, on any matters 
of interpretation in this section shall be final. 

 
E. D. 
Planning and development department staff shall provide reports on prior fiscal 
year program activity to the governing body not less than annually. The reports shall 
contain: 
 
1. The total number of projects utilizing the program;. 
 
2. The total amount of fees waived;. and 
 
3. The total valuation of projects incentivized by the program. 
 
F. E. 
The total amount of building permit fees waived reimbursed under this section shall 
be capped at one hundred thousand dollars ($100,000.00) for each fiscal year in accordance 
with the limitations imposed under this section and as determined by the director of the 
planning and development director. department. Reimbursements shall be limited as 
follows:  
 
1. Not more than $200,000 for the entire program; 
 
2. Not more than $100,000 for downtown core projects; 
 
3. Not more than $150,000 for affordable housing projects; and 
 
4. Not more than $20,000 for historic rehabilitation projects.  
 
F. A decision from the director of the planning and development department, or their 
designee, on any matters of interpretation in this section shall be final. 
 
G. The provisions of Section 15.08.031 of this chapter shall be in effect until June 30, 2025.

Honorable Mayor Collins and Esteemed Cheyenne City Council 
2101 O’Neil Ave. 
Cheyenne, WY  82001 
Recommendation Number Four, Fee Waiver Program 
Dear Mayor Collins and Members of City Council, 
We the members of the Affordable Housing Task Force recommend the Mayor’s Office and City 
Council/City Staff immediately start creating a Fee Waiver/Reimbursement Program for   
developers/builders to incentivize the building of affordable housing. The program would waive the 
impact and/or building permit fee for affordable housing developments that are income restricted to 
those who earn at or below 80% of AMI.  The waiver/reimbursement program will also be available for 
those seeking to build shelters or other projects that benefit people experiencing homelessness.  
We anticipate this program being very similar to the fee waiver/reimbursement program that is 
currently in place for downtown development. The Fee Waiver application would be due at the time of 
submission of the development to the planning commission for their review and verification that the 
development will be income restricted.  
 
Respectfully, 
 
Brenda L. Birkle, Chair

STAFF REPORT 
 
FILE:

REQUEST: 
A request to zone approximately 4.84 acres of land from MUB Mixed-use 
Business Emphasis to P Public

2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282 
(Fax) 307-637-6366 

UDC-23-98 
 
 
September 5, 2023 
RECOMMENDED MOTION: 
 
Move to approve the zoning of Lot 1, Block 1, Back 40 Subdivision, 3rd Filing, Laramie County, Wyoming 
to P Public District as shown in the attached zone change map, noting that the project meets the review 
criteria for approval. 
 
APPLICABLE CITY CODE SECTION(S) AND PLANS: 
 
• 
UDC 2.2.1 Zoning Map Amendment 
• 
UDC Article 5 
• 
Missile Drive Corridor Plan (2010) 
• 
PlanCheyenne 
 
BACKGROUND: 
 
This zone change application proposes changing the zoning from MUB Mixed-use Business Emphasis P Public 
for roughly 4.84 acres of land. This zone change is for the development of a public facility. Some of the land 
directly to the south is currently going through a relatively unrelated zone change from CB to MUB (although 
both projects are partnering on and utilizing new street and drainage infrastructure, the properties subject to 
the zone change requests are being done by two different parties for two different purposes).  
 
SURROUNDING LAND USE AND ZONING: 
 
 
ANALYSIS OF APPLICATION CONFORMANCE WITH REVIEW CRITERIA: 
 
Should the Planning Commission wish to recommend approval of the Zoning Map Amendment, the 
Commission must make the following findings required in UDC 2.2.1.d. A detailed analysis is below: 
 
1) The application is in accordance with the development policies and physical patterns 
identified in the Comprehensive Plan.  (UDC 2.2.1.d.1) 
 
PlanCheyenne encourages growth within the Urban Service Boundary and the maximization of 
existing infrastructure. This property is within the Urban Service Boundary, in an area with 
existing infrastructure.  
 
Future Land Use 
Designation

UDC-23-98 
 
 
September 5, 2023 
The Future Land Use Designation for this property and most surrounding properties is Mixed 
Use Residential. Surrounding the area is also the Mixed Use Commercial designation. These 
designations encourage a mix of residential and commercial uses with associated zone districts. 
According to comprehensive plan the optimal City zone for the subject property’s future land 
use designation is Mixed-use Residential, but a variety of commercial or residential zones may 
be applicable to meet the intended development pattern. While the future land use map of 
PlanCheyenne would suggest a commercial or residential zoning for the property, public uses 
such as parks, schools, and recreation facilities can be appropriate in nearly any zoning district, 
serving residents and businesses either nearby or regionally. It is hard to predict where public 
uses will be needed or where such public-use development is feasible. Although there is a 
‘public’ designation on the future land use map, it is largely only used for existing public (or 
quasi-public) facilities. 
 
PlanCheyenne encourages the measured growth of public facilities to keep up with the needs 
and demands of a growing community. Public investment is encouraged along the west 
Lincolnway corridor to encourage private investment. The creation of public facilities is one form 
of public investment that can assist in this objective. Quality of life amenities (including 
recreation-center-like facilities) are encouraged to be competitive with front range facilities. 
 
The proposed zone change complies with review criterion 1. 
 
2) The application is compatible with nearby property, specifically considering existing zoning, 
the Comprehensive Plan, and any other official plans and policies created under the guidance 
of that plan for these areas; (UDC 2.2.1.d.2) 
 
Nearby properties are zoned LI, CB and MUB. Due to the nature of the P zone, it is frequently 
found in areas all by itself for public facilities that are surrounded by private developments. 
Generally speaking, the P zone is appropriate anywhere in the community where a public land 
use is present, as such, the P zone is largely compatible with all zone districts and 
comprehensive plan designations. 
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with review criterion 2. 
   
3) The suitability of the subject property for development according to the standards and uses of 
the existing zoning district, compared to the suitability of the subject property for 
development according to the standards and uses of the proposed zoning district (UDC 
2.2.1.d.3) 
  
The existing zone district allows for a variety of residential and commercial uses. The P district 
allows for a variety of public uses. While the requested P zone has significantly fewer allowed 
uses, the property is owned by the City, and public uses are anticipated for the property.   
 
The proposed zone change complies with review criterion 3. 
 
4) The ability of the subject property and surrounding vicinity to be developed according to the 
Subdivision Standards in Article 4 considering the proposed zoning (UDC 2.2.1.d.4) 
 
Although the underlying comprehensive plan designations would suggest a smaller block size, 
due to the varied public uses Article 4 has a specific exemption for civic uses in regards to block 
size. The property has multiple possible access points and could also share access with 

UDC-23-98 
 
 
September 5, 2023 
neighboring properties. The Master Transportation Plan’s collector and arterial street network 
would still apply, but the property is already close to major streets and no new major streets 
would be expected to go through this parcel. 
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with review criterion 4. 
 
5) The ability of the application to support the Intent and Applicability statements of the 
proposed zoning district and achieve the Design Objectives of any specific standards. (UDC 
2.2.1.d.5) 
 
The AG zone is intended to develop with a variety of agricultural uses, in areas far from City 
services or as a holding zone until future development occurs. The zone does not have any zone-
specific design standards. 
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with review criterion 5. 
    
6) The ability of all potential future development to meet all applicable standards for 
development in the proposed zoning district, including other development options if any 
specific development proposed in association with the application is not executed after 
approval. (UDC 2.2.1.d.6) 
 
The specific development proposed with this zone change is a gymnastics facility. The P zone is 
more permissive in design standards and parking requirements than the current MUB zone. 
Should the current proposed use not be executed, it is probable another civic use would be 
installed at the site. Should a non-governmental agency take control of the parcel, a zone 
change away from P will be required. 
 
The proposed zone change complies with review criterion 6. 
 
7) The review and recommendations of other reviewing bodies. (UDC 2.2.1.d.7) 
 
No reviewing agencies provided recommendations regarding the proposed zone change.  
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with review criterion 7. 
 
8) Whether the application will harm the public health, safety, or general welfare. (UDC 
2.2.1.d.8) 
 
City staff is satisfied that this proposed zone change would not adversely affect the public 
health, safety, or general welfare. 
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with review criterion 8. 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 

UDC-23-98 
 
 
September 5, 2023 
 
CONFORMANCE WITH APPLICABLE PLANS: 
 
The project is located in an area covered by the following plans: 
• 
PlanCheyenne 
• 
Missile Drive Corridor Plan (2010) 
Conformance with PlanCheyenne is covered by review criterion 1.   
 
The Missile Drive Corridor Plan (2010) examine the potential redevelopment of the area. It suggested 
revamping the street network for better connectivity and expansion of an ‘entertainment district’ 
focused on the Ice and Events Center. The Back 40 Subdivision 3rd Filing Plat helped establish street 
network connectivity within the current constraints. The proposed gymnastics facility is considered an 
entertainment venue and would expand the entertainment district at this location in conformance with 
this plans’ recommendations.   
 
STAFF REVIEW: 
 
Through the review process, no review comments were received. 
 
CONCLUSION: 
 
Staff is satisfied that the application meets the review criteria set forth in UDC 2.2.1.(d). The P zone is 
appropriate based on the current ownership and the desire to use the property as a public facility. 

UDC-23-98 
 
 
September 5, 2023 
 
ALTERNATIVES: 
 
1. Recommend approval of the zoning amendment as submitted by the applicant (Staff 
Recommendation).  
2. Recommend approval of the zoning amendment subject to conditions or modifications.  
3. Recommend denial of the zoning amendment based on findings of denial. The Planning 
Commission must conclude that the project does not meet all the review criteria. As part of the 
motion, findings for denial must be stated. 
4. Postpone the zoning amendment until issues identified during the meeting can be resolved. 
 
General Information Regarding Alternatives: 
 
If the Commission acts contrary to staff’s recommendation or modifies conditions of approval, the 
Commission shall cite specific reasons (based on the review criteria) for approval or denial. 
 
STAFF RECOMMENDATION: 
 
Staff recommends approval of the zoning of the property P Public District. 
 
ATTACHMENTS: 
1. Current Zoning Map 
2. Future Land Use Map 
3. Master Application 
4. Applicant Provided Zone Change Map 
5. Recorded Back 40 Subdivision, 3rd Filing plat map (for property reference)

ORDINANCE NO. ____________________ 
ENTITLED: “AN ORDINANCE AMENDING THE OFFICIAL ZONING MAP OF THE 
CITY OF CHEYENNE CHANGING THE ZONING CLASSIFICATION 
FROM CB COMMUNITY BUSINESS TO MUB MIXED-USE BUSINESS 
FOR LAND LOCATED ON LOTS 3, 4, 5, 6, 7, AND 8, BLOCK 1, HITCHING 
POST PLAZA, 3RD FILING.” 
BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, 
WYOMING: 
Section 1. That the owner(s) of the property described herein have applied for a zoning 
map amendment (“zone change”) for said property in accordance with Section 2.2.1, Zoning Map 
Amendment, of the Cheyenne Unified Development Code (“UDC”). 
Section 2. That on September 5, 2023, the City of Cheyenne Planning Commission 
conducted a public hearing, reviewed the proposed zone change that is the subject of this 
ordinance, and recommended approval of said zone change. 
Section 3. That the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne finds that the zone change 
that is the subject of this ordinance meets the criteria specified in Section 2.2.1.d of the UDC. 
Section 4. That, in accordance with Section 2.2.1, Zoning Map Amendment, Section 5.1.2, 
Zoning Districts Established, and Section 5.1.3, Official Zoning Map, of the UDC, the 
aforementioned application is hereby approved and the zoning classification of the real property 
described as Lots 3, 4, 5, 6, 7, & 8, Block 1, Hitching Post Plaza, 3rd Filing is hereby changed to 
MUB Mixed-Use Business. 
Section 5. That the City Engineer is hereby authorized and directed to alter and amend the 
Official Zoning Map as described in Section 5.1.3 of the UDC to comply with the zone change as 
described in Section 4 above when this ordinance takes effect in accordance with Section 7 below. 
Section 6. Sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses and phrases of this ordinance are 
severable. If any section, paragraph, sentence, clause, or phrase of this ordinance is declared 
unconstitutional, such unconstitutionality shall not affect any of the remaining sections, 
paragraphs, sentences, clauses, and phrases. 
Section 7. That this ordinance shall be in full force and effect upon its approval and 
publication, and upon the official filing of the affiliated Hitching Post Plaza, 3rd Filing final plat 
map with the County Clerk and Ex-Officio Register of Deeds for Laramie County, Wyoming.

CITY OF CHEYENNE PLANNING COMMISSION 
SEPTEMBER 5, 2023 
STAFF REPORT 
FILE: 
PUDC-23-103:  Hitching Post Plaza, 3rd Filing – Zoning Map Amendment 
(zone change)  
REQUEST: 
A request to rezone approximately 4.55 acres of land from 
CB Community Business to MUB Mixed-Use Business  
LOCATION: 
Lots 3, 4, 5, 6, 7, & 8, Block 1, Hitching Post Plaza, 3rd Filing 
APPLICANT: 
Casey Palma, Palma Land Planning LLC 
AGENT: 
Casey Palma, Palma Land Planning LLC 
OWNER: 
1600 Hitching Post, LLC 
ZONING: 
CB Community Business 
FUTURE LAND USE: 
Mixed-Use Commercial and Mixed-Use Residential 
PURPOSE: 
To allow for smaller setbacks and more residential development options 
PREPARED BY: 
Lonnie Olson, Planner I  
Planning and Development Department 
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282 
(Fax) 307-637-6366 

PUDC-23-103 
 
 
September 5, 2023 
RECOMMENDED MOTION: 
 
Move to approve the rezoning of Lots 3, 4, 5, 6, 7, & 8, Block 1, Hitching Post Plaza, 3rd Filing to MUB 
Mixed-Use Business as shown in the attached zone change map, noting that the project meets the 
review criteria for approval. 
 
APPLICABLE CITY CODE SECTION(S) AND PLANS: 
 
• 
UDC 2.2.1 Zoning Map Amendment 
• 
UDC Article 5 
• 
PlanCheyenne 
 
BACKGROUND: 
 
This zone change application proposes changing the zoning from CB Community Business to MUB Mixed-Use 
Business for 4.55 acres of vacant land. The properties are anticipated to have a variety of commercial uses 
and possible housing on the north of the property. One of the properties to the north has a zoning map 
amendment being considered to change the zoning from Mixed-Use Business to Public for the new 
gymnastics facility. 
 
SURROUNDING LAND USE AND ZONING: 
 
 
ANALYSIS OF APPLICATION CONFORMANCE WITH REVIEW CRITERIA: 
 
Should the Planning Commission wish to recommend approval of the Zoning Map Amendment, the 
Commission must make the following findings required in UDC 2.2.1.d. A detailed analysis is below: 
 
1) The application is in accordance with the development policies and physical patterns 
identified in the Comprehensive Plan.  (UDC 2.2.1.d.1) 
 
PlanCheyenne encourages growth within the Urban Service Boundary and the maximization of 
existing infrastructure. This property is within the Urban Service Boundary, in an area with 
existing infrastructure. Plan Cheyenne also encourages infill development. 
 
Future Land Use Designation

Site 
Mixed-Use Commercial 
and Mixed-Use

PUDC-23-103 
 
 
September 5, 2023 
The Future Land Use Designation for this property is Mixed-Use Commercial and Mixed-Use 
Residential and the surrounding area is Mixed-Use Commercial and Mixed-Use Residential. 
According to the comprehensive plan the optimal City zones for the subject property’s future 
land use designations is MUB Mixed-Use Business and MUR Mixed-Use Residential, although a 
variety of commercial, residential, and mixed-use zones could also be appropriate based on the 
context of the site. 
 
The proposed zone change complies with review criterion 1. 
 
2) The application is compatible with nearby property, specifically considering existing zoning, 
the Comprehensive Plan, and any other official plans and policies created under the guidance 
of that plan for these areas; (UDC 2.2.1.d.2) 
 
Nearby properties to the north are zoned MUB. This is the same zone that is proposed with this 
zoning map amendment.  
 
Nearby properties to the north, south, east, and west are zoned CB. Some of these properties 
are a part of a commercial corridor along a principal arterial. The Comprehensive Plan 
encourages commercial activities which serve neighborhoods, the community, and region. The 
Comprehensive Plan encourages the blending of CB zones with nearby neighborhoods in terms 
of scale, design, signage, and lighting. The MUB zone is intended for a diverse mix of residential, 
retail, service, employment and civic uses integrated into a walkable compact development 
pattern.  
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with review criterion 2. 
   
3) The suitability of the subject property for development according to the standards and uses of 
the existing zoning district, compared to the suitability of the subject property for 
development according to the standards and uses of the proposed zoning district (UDC 
2.2.1.d.3) 
  
The current CB zone allows a variety of commercial uses but prohibits residential uses and 
requires large setbacks from streets. The proposed MUB zone allows a variety of commercial 
uses, residential uses, and mixed uses. The MUB zone also allows buildings to be built to the 
street. The MUB zone has a higher design standard than the CB zone; however, the subject 
property should be able to meet the required the design standards. 
 
The proposed zone change complies with review criterion 3. 
 
4) The ability of the subject property and surrounding vicinity to be developed according to the 
Subdivision Standards in Article 4 considering the proposed zoning (UDC 2.2.1.d.4) 
 
The existing block is the type expected for the urban-style of development anticipated for MUB 
zoning. CB zoning typically anticipates larger suburban-style blocks. The proposed pattern with 
the associated plat is compatible with the proposed zoning and no further subdivision is 
expected. 
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with review criterion 4. 
 

PUDC-23-103 
 
 
September 5, 2023 
5) The ability of the application to support the Intent and Applicability statements of the 
proposed zoning district and achieve the Design Objectives of any specific standards. (UDC 
2.2.1.d.5) 
 
The MUB zone is intended for a diverse mix of residential, retail, service, employment and civic 
uses integrated into a walkable, compact development pattern. The MUB District is applicable to 
areas where more intense development and diverse mix of uses is appropriate with a high level 
of pedestrian and civic amenities. 
 
The existing development patterns, including existing uses, are reflective of the intent and 
applicability of the MUB zone. Commercial and Civic uses are within a short distance of the 
subject property.  
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with review criterion 5. 
    
6) The ability of all potential future development to meet all applicable standards for 
development in the proposed zoning district, including other development options if any 
specific development proposed in association with the application is not executed after 
approval. (UDC 2.2.1.d.6) 
 
The existing property meets the standards of the MUB zone. The MUB zone allows a building to 
be brought closer to the street, while a new building on this property in the CB zone would 
require 25’ setbacks from both streets. 
 
The proposed zone change complies with review criterion 6. 
 
7) The review and recommendations of other reviewing bodies. (UDC 2.2.1.d.7) 
 
No reviewing agencies provided recommendations regarding the proposed zone change. 
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with review criterion 7. 
 
8) Whether the application will harm the public health, safety, or general welfare. (UDC 
2.2.1.d.8) 
 
City staff is satisfied that this proposed zone change would not adversely affect the public 
health, safety, or general welfare. 
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with review criterion 8. 
 
CONFORMANCE WITH APPLICABLE PLANS: 
 
The project is located in an area covered by the following plans: 
• 
PlanCheyenne 
Conformance with PlanCheyenne is covered by review criterion 1.     
 
STAFF REVIEW: 
 
Through the review process the following comments were received: 
• 
Concerns with the southwestern lots meeting applicability of the MUB zone 

PUDC-23-103 
 
 
September 5, 2023 
• 
Concerns with meeting the intent of the Urban Use zone 
 
The revised zoning map submitted on August 24, 2023 addressed staff’s comments. 
 
 
CONCLUSION: 
 
Staff is satisfied that the application meets the review criteria set forth in UDC 2.2.1.(d).  The MUB 
Mixed-Use Business is appropriate based on the future land use map, surrounding zoning, and 
surrounding development patterns. 
 
ALTERNATIVES: 
 
1. Recommend approval of the zoning amendment as submitted by the applicant (Staff 
Recommendation).  
2. Recommend approval of the zoning amendment subject to conditions or modifications.  
3. Recommend denial of the zoning amendment based on findings of denial. The Planning 
Commission must conclude that the project does not meet all the review criteria. As part of the 
motion, findings for denial must be stated. 
4. Postpone the zoning amendment until issues identified during the meeting can be resolved. 
 
General Information Regarding Alternatives: 
 
If the Commission acts contrary to staff’s recommendation or modifies conditions of approval, the 
Commission shall cite specific reasons (based on the review criteria) for approval or denial. 
 
STAFF RECOMMENDATION: 
 
Staff recommends approval of the rezoning of the property from CB to MUB  
 
ATTACHMENTS: 
1. Current Zoning Map 
2. Future Land Use Map 
3. Master Application 
4. Applicant Provided Zone Change Maps

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2023-09-05_TotalExtraction_24693242.pdf
 
ORDINANCE NO. ________ 
 
ENTITLED: "AN ORDINANCE AMENDING THE OFFICIAL ZONING MAP OF THE 
CITY OF CHEYENNE CHANGING THE ZONING CLASSIFICATION 
FROM CB COMMUNITY BUSINESS TO MUB MIXED-USE BUSINESS 
FOR LAND LOCATED ON LOTS 3, 4, 5, AND 6, BLOCK 1, HITCHING 
POST PLAZA, 3RD FILING." 
 
 
BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, 
WYOMING: 
 
 
Section 1.  That the owner(s) of the property described herein have applied for a zoning 
map amendment (“zone change”) for said property in accordance with Section 2.2.1, Zoning Map 
Amendment, of the Cheyenne Unified Development Code (“UDC”). 
 
 
Section 2.  That on September 5, 2023, the City of Cheyenne Planning Commission 
conducted a public hearing, reviewed the proposed zone change that is the subject of this 
ordinance, and recommended approval of said zone change. 
 
 
Section 3.  That the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne finds that the zone change 
that is the subject of this ordinance meets the criteria specified in Section 2.2.1.d of the UDC. 
 
 
Section 4.  That, in accordance with Section 2.2.1, Zoning Map Amendment, Section 5.1.2, 
Zoning Districts Established, and Section 5.1.3, Official Zoning Map, of the UDC, the 
aforementioned application is hereby approved and the zoning classification of the real property 
described as Lots 3, 4, 5, & 6, Block 1, Hitching Post Plaza, 3rd Filing is hereby changed to MUB 
Mixed-Use Business. 
 
 
Section 5.  That the City Engineer is hereby authorized and directed to alter and amend the 
Official Zoning Map as described in Section 5.1.3 of the UDC to comply with the zone change as 
described in Section 4 above when this ordinance takes effect in accordance with Section 7 below. 
 
 
Section 6.  Sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses and phrases of this ordinance are 
severable. If any section, paragraph, sentence, clause, or phrase of this ordinance is declared 
unconstitutional, such unconstitutionality shall not affect any of the remaining sections, 
paragraphs, sentences, clauses, and phrases. 
 
 
Section 7.  That this ordinance shall be in full force and effect upon its approval and 
publication, and upon the official filing of the affiliated Hitching Post Plaza, 3rd Filing final plat 
map with the County Clerk and Ex-Officio Register of Deeds for Laramie County, Wyoming.

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2023-09-05_TotalExtraction_73140839.pdf
 
 
Board of Public Utilities 
Cheyenne Water and Sewer Departments 
 
 
 
2416 Snyder Ave. 
Cheyenne, Wyoming 82001 
(307) 637-6460 
www.cheyennebopu.org 
 
 
 
MEMORANDUM 
 
 
To: 
 Honorable Mayor Patrick Collins and Members of the City Council 
 
From: 
Brad Brooks, Director, Board of Public Utilities 
 
Subject: 
Ordinance amending city code provisions governing wastewater treatment and 
disposal 
 
Date: 
September 6, 2023 
 
 
Introduction: 
The proposed ordinance governs the treatment and disposal of wastewater.  This chapter is required 
by and subject to approval by the United States Environmental Protection Agency (USEPA).  The 
changes to this ordinance have been passed by the Cheyenne Board of Public Utilities Board and 
have been deemed approvable by the USEPA following approval by the Cheyenne Governing 
Body and the USEPA’s public comment period which will follow. 
 
Discussion: 
A list of changes to the ordinance has been included with this packet.  Most of the changes that 
you will find throughout the ordinance are to create agreement with other areas of the ordinance 
or to correct grammatical errors.  The sections that we would like to discuss today are 13.20.730 
and 13.20.740.   
 
In sections 13.20.730 and 13.20.740, the BOPU hopes to clarify and expedite a process for 
enforcing upon repeat offenders.  Our goal is to help our industrial users to gain compliance.   
By the time an industrial user gets to a Termination of Service, as described in these sections, they 
have likely been in violation five or more times.  We have made repeated efforts to educate the 
industrial user and to encourage compliance to the best of our abilities and according to our 
Enforcement Response Plan, which has also been approved by the USEPA.  In the enforcement 

 
Page 2 of 2 
realm, we have four avenues to explore.  The first is written communication.  This is typically our 
first several responses to noncompliance, it may include an inspection, correspondence, Notice of 
Violations, or Administrative Compliance Orders.  Secondly, we have Administrative Fines.  We 
use this option often in conjunction with written communication.  Our goal is to set fines at a fair 
value for the severity or repetition of a violation. Third, we have court actions available in our 
toolbox.  This is an action we prefer not to use due to the timelines and cost.  We feel that, except 
in particular circumstances, this is not the best use of ratepayer money.  Our final tool is 
Termination of Service.  Though we try to avoid this situation, when industrial users are repeat 
offenders, we believe this is the best avenue to achieve compliance.  As we mentioned earlier, this 
code is approved by the USEPA.  The USEPA audits the BOPU regularly to make sure the city 
code is being followed and enforced upon.  The USEPA also requires that enforcement be escalated 
for repeat offenders.  The two ways the BOPU has to escalate is to either increase Administrative 
Fines or to Terminate Service.  We believe that after several failed attempts to gain compliance by 
Administrative Fines, it is necessary to proceed to the Termination of Service.  It establishes a 
strong precedent and less of a financial impact on the repeat offender, especially for our smaller 
local businesses, to terminate service for a small period of time than to continue to increase fines.  
My staff and I would be open to discussing this with you at any time. 

I. 
PROJECT: Chapter 13.20 Public Services, Wastewater Treatment and Disposal 
Chapter Revision 
 
II. 
GENERAL DISCRIPTION AND PURPOSE:  Chapter 13.20 of the City of Cheyenne 
Code governs the Public Services, Wastewater Treatment and Disposal.  The 
BOPU staff has reviewed, updated, and revised the existing code sections 
within this chapter.  The majority of changes are to create agreement and 
clarity with the remainder of the Chapter or with updated industry 
terminology.  The most significant changes come in Section 13.20.730 and 740 
to clarify how to utilize the enforcement remedy of Termination of Service as 
an end when an industrial user has a long history of noncompliance and other 
attempted remedies of noncompliance have not solved the cycle of 
noncompliance. 
As the Control Authority for the EPA, we must be able to use the ordinance as 
a tool to implement federal rules and to effectively protect the sanitary sewer 
infrastructure, our reclamation facilities, the people who work on or around 
the system and the receiving waters that our plants discharge to.  We believe 
that providing this clarity in the code will provide a fair yet firm way to deal 
with industrial users that have a long history of noncompliance, especially in 
our Sector Control area. 
III. 
FINANCIAL: No financial impact. 
 
IV. 
STATUS: To date, BOPU staff, EPA Region 8 staff, and the BOPU Attorney have 
approved revisions.  The document has been approved by the BOPU Board 
and is now awaiting City Governing Body approval. 
 
V. 
REQUEST AND RECOMMENDATIONS: The BOPU recommends that the City 
Governing Body approve revisions to the Chapter 13.20 Public Services – 
Wastewater Treatment and Disposal Chapter Revision subject to final 
approval by the EPA after their public comment period.
