PUDC-23-99  
 
 
September 5, 2023 
ANALYSIS OF APPLICATION CONFORMANCE WITH REVIEW CRITERIA: 
 
Should the Planning Commission wish to recommend approval of the Preliminary Plat, the Commission 
must make the following findings required in UDC 2.1.2.d  – a detailed analysis is below: 
 
1) The application is in accordance with the development policies and physical patterns 
identified in the Comprehensive Plan. (UDC 2.1.2.d.1) 
 
This development is in conformance with the principles and future land uses identified in 
PlanCheyenne. The property is within the Urban Service Boundary and has access to all utilities. 
The future land use map within PlanCheyenne identifies this area as developing with residential. 
PlanCheyenne encourages a mix of residential types. The applicant is proposing lots to be 
developed with single-family.  
 
The application complies with this review criterion.  
 
2) The application reflects sound planning and urban design principles with respect to existing 
and potential future development in the vicinity, including consideration of current and future 
streets, open spaces, blocks and lots, and public utilities or facilities. (UDC 2.1.2.d.2) 
 
This plat is one of the MDR zones of the Sweetgrass PUD. The MDR is intended to provide for 
residential development allowing for a variety of single-family detached and attached dwelling 
units, located nearby to schools and commercial centers. An extensive open space network 
should be provided within each MDR parcel. To promote greater diversity, lower density 
development, such as that permitted within LDR districts, is permitted within MDR districts. 
 
The proposed plat does not change the street layout that has already been platted. It does 
propose reducing the width of Sirocco Drive to a local road width. A future extension of Ledoux 
Drive is proposed to create another connection at a later date.    
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
   
3) The division of land meets the purposes, intent, and all applicable standards in Article 4. (UDC 
2.1.2.d.3) 
 
Community Facility Fees (4.2): 
For fees based on acreage, the area of the plat is pro-rated to the nearest tenth of an acre. The 
required fees for the 8.4 acres of non-exempt land in this plat are as follows: 
Land Acquisition Fee  
 
 
 
$2,940.00 ($350 per acre) 
Infrastructure Fee   
 
 
 
$2,940.00 ($350 per acre) 
Total Land Acquisition & Infrastructure Fees 
$5,880.00 
Per UDC Section 4.2.5, Table 4-2, the Land Acquisition & Infrastructure fee are payable at 
the time of the signing of the final plat.  
 
Per UDC Section 4.2.5, Table 4-2, Enhancement Fees are payable at the issuance of a 
building permit for each structure. 
Residential Enhancement Fee 
$400 per dwelling unit 
 
 

PUDC-23-99  
 
 
September 5, 2023 
Community Facility Fees were only calculated for Block 16 because when it was originally 
platted it was designated as a lot for a school and did not have any fees at the time. Block 15 has 
already been paid for with the previous plat. 
 
 
Transportation Network (4.3): 
This is a replat of an area that has previously been approved. The preliminary plat does propose 
extending Ledoux Drive to connect into Railspike Road. Sirocco Drive is proposed to be 
narrowed and reduced to a local right-of-way width. The original network is not being changed 
with the final plat.  
 
Open Space (4.4): 
An open space parcel is provided on this plat. There are also open space easements proposed. 
This track and easements are part of Sweetgrass PUD trail system. 
 
Required Improvements (4.5): 
This plat appears to be able to provide all required utilities to the lots within this development. 
 
Public Safety Fees (4.6): 
Public Safety Fees are required at $949.37 per dwelling unit. Per UDC Section 4.6.3.b, Public 
Safety Fees are payable at the issuance of a building permit for each structure.  
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
 
4) Any phasing proposed in the application is clearly indicated and demonstrates a logical and 
well-planned development pattern. This may include consideration of amenities and public 
facilities of the subdivision within each phase compared to the anticipated timing or the 
likelihood of future phases being constructed as planned. (UDC 2.1.2.d.4) 
 
No specific phasing is proposed with this project. 
 
Review criterion not applicable.   
 
5) The impacts identified in specific studies or technical reports that were prepared or requested 
to be prepared in association with the application, and whether the findings and 
recommendations of these reports, or any modifications or conditions on approval of the 
application, can mitigate these impacts. (UDC 2.1.2.d.5) 
 
Drainage: 
A Final Stormwater Management Plan for Phase 1 of Sweetgrass was submitted and reviewed. 
There were no concerns with drainage at the plat level for the project as presented.   
 
Traffic: 
A Transportation Impact Study was submitted and reviewed. There were no concerns with 
traffic at the plat level for the project as presented.   
 
The application complies with this review criterion. 
  
 
 

PUDC-23-99  
 
 
September 5, 2023 
6) Lots proposed for development shall be buildable lots according to the existing or proposed 
zoning district standards, result in design and development patterns that meet the Intent and 
Applicability statements of the existing or proposed zoning district, and not aggravate 
nonconforming setbacks or currently adopted building and fire code access and separation 
requirements. (UDC 2.1.2.d.6) 
 
The proposed plat is one of the MDR zones of the PUD. The MDR is intended to provide for 
residential development allowing for a variety of single-family detached and attached dwelling 
units, located nearby to schools and commercial centers. An extensive open space network 
should be provided with within each MDR parcel. To promote greater diversity, lower density 
development, such as that permitted within LDR districts, is permitted within MDR districts. All 
lots proposed for development are buildable lots in this zone district. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
 
7) All parcels not proposed as buildable lots and which are not dedicated to and accepted by the 
City shall have a clearly defined entity responsible for ongoing maintenance.  (UDC 2.1.2.d.7) 
 
The PUD requires common lots to be owned, developed, improved, managed, and/or 
maintained by homeowners’ associations, architectural review board(s), community 
development districts, and/or similar entities (PUD Section 1, Subsection G). 
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
 
8) The application will not create any undue influence on existing or potential development for 
adjacent property considering the existing zoning and Comprehensive Plan policies for that 
property.  (UDC 2.1.2.d.8) 
 
This plat is in conformance with the design and layout anticipated for this parcel by the 
Sweetgrass PUD. Adjacent Sweetgrass developments were designed and laid out with this 
development as an integral part of the broader development pattern.  
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
 
9) The application is not harmful to the public health, safety, and welfare. (UDC 2.1.2.d.9) 
 
City staff is satisfied that this proposed plat would not adversely affect the public health, safety, 
or general welfare. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
 
10) Remainder lot(s) shall be clearly identified on the preliminary plat and shall not be developed 
or proposed for development for purposes.  Remainder lot(s) shall be noted and described on 
all plats either graphically or by legal description. (UDC 2.1.2.d.10) 
 
Remainder lots are not proposed with this plat. 
 
Review criterion not applicable. 
 

PUDC-23-99  
 
 
September 5, 2023 
11) The layout and design of the final plat is in substantial compliance with the approved 
preliminary plat considering the number of lots or parcels, street and block layout, and access, 
and any deviations in the final plat brings the application in further compliance with the 
Comprehensive Plan. (UDC 2.1.3.d.1) 
 
The Final Plat submitted with the application is substantially similar to the Preliminary Plat 
submitted. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
 
12) The application meets any conditions or modifications of the approved preliminary plat. (UDC 
2.1.3.d.2) 
 
Through the review process and application of the Preliminary Plat, Staff does not recommend 
any conditions to the plat.  
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
   
13) The application meets the purposes, intent, and applicable standards of Article 4. (UDC 
2.1.3.d.3) 
 
As a concurrent Preliminary and Final Plat application, this Final Plat review criterion is covered 
by the equivalent Preliminary Plat review criterion (subsection 3 above). 
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
 
CONFORMANCE WITH APPLICABLE PLANS: 
The project is located in an area covered by the following plans: 
• 
PlanCheyenne 
Conformance with PlanCheyenne is covered by review criterion 1. 
 
STAFF REVIEW: 
 
Through the review process, comments were received regarding the following issues: 
1. Minor clerical corrections (corrected with revised submittal) 
2. The clarity of block labels 
Note: the change to the block labels to clearly include text stating ‘Block’ is recommended by 
both the County Clerk and the Development office.  
 
CONCLUSION: 
 
Staff analysis is that the application meets the review criteria and therefore recommends approval of 
the expedited plat application as submitted by the applicant.  
 
The area this plat is proposed in is anticipated to develop as a largely residential neighborhood, based 
on both existing development and the patterns proposed by the comprehensive plan. The proposed plat 
is consistent with this development pattern.   
 
 
  

PUDC-23-99  
 
 
September 5, 2023 
ALTERNATIVES: 
 
1. Recommend approval of the expedited preliminary plat, forwarding the Final Plat to the 
Governing Body. 
2. Recommend approval of the preliminary plat with conditions and that a final plat addressing the 
conditions of approval be prepared and presented to the Governing Body.  
3. Recommend approval of the preliminary plat as submitted by the applicant and forward the 
preliminary plat to the Governing Body. 
4. Recommend approval of the preliminary plat subject to staff’s recommended conditions and 
forward the preliminary plat to the Governing Body.  
5. Recommend denial of the preliminary plat based on findings of denial. The Planning Commission 
must conclude that the project does not meet all the review criteria. As part of the motion, 
findings for denial must be stated. 
6. Postpone the project until issues identified during the meeting can be resolved. 
 
General Information Regarding Alternatives: 
The Planning Commission may modify staff’s recommended conditions, delete conditions, add 
conditions, or decide contrary to staff’s recommendation. In the event conditions are modified, the 
conditions shall relate to applicable review criteria. In the event that the Commission acts contrary to 
staff’s recommendation or modifies conditions of approval, the Commission shall cite specific reasons 
(based on the review criteria) for approval or denial. 
 
STAFF RECOMMENDATION: 
 
Staff recommends approval of the expedited Preliminary Plat, advancing the Final Plat to the Governing 
Body. 
 
ATTACHMENTS: 
1. Zoning Map  
2. Land Use Plan Map 
3. Master Application Form 
4. Preliminary Plat 
5. Final Plat

MEMO 
To: 
Honorable Mayor Patrick Collins and Members of the City Council 
From: 
Connor White, Planner II 
Subject: PUDC-23-100 / R and R at Sweetgrass / Final Plat  
Date: 
September 11, 2023 
 
Background:  
The item for consideration is a Final Plat. This is approved by Resolution.    
 
The proposed Governing Body review schedule is as follows: 
• 
Introduction and Referral 9/11/2023 
• 
Public Services Committee 9/19/2023 
• 
Final consideration 9/25/2023 
 
Planning Commission Recommendation:  
The Planning Commission heard this item at their September 5, 2023 meeting and unanimously voted to 
recommend the Governing Body approve this application with staffs conditions. 
 
Staffs conditions are as follow: 
1. Prior to Governing Body approval: 
a. Modify the curves on the Prairie Gold Lane right-of-way so the centerline meets 
AASHTO standards for a minimum 30mph design speed. 
b. Number the blocks, and renumber or rename the lots appropriately.   
  
The Planning Commission Staff report is attached.  
 
Attachments: 
1. Resolution 
2. Final Map 
3. Planning Commission Staff Report 
  
  
  
  
  
  
  
  Planning and Development Department 
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282 (Fax) 307-637-6366 

 
RESOLUTION NO. __________ 
 
ENTITLED: "A RESOLUTION AUTHORIZING THE MAYOR AND THE CITY 
CLERK TO SIGN A FINAL PLAT FOR R AND R AT SWEETGRASS, A 
REPLAT OF ALL OF LOTS 1, 2, & 3, AND TRACT T, BLOCK 8, 
SWEETGRASS, 2ND FILING, AND A PORTION OF ADJACENT 
RIGHTS-OF-WAY, CITY OF CHEYENNE, LARAMIE COUNTY, 
WYOMING (LOCATED SOUTHEAST OF THE INTERSECTION OF 
SWEETGRASS DRIVE AND E COLLEGE DRIVE.)." 
 
 
WHEREAS, Sweetgrass Land CO, LLC has subdivided said land in accordance with the 
statutes in such cases made and provided; 
 
 
WHEREAS, Sweetgrass Land CO, LLC the owner of the above described land, has caused 
a subdivision plat of said land to be made, acknowledged, and certified, particularly describing the 
lots, blocks, easements, and rights-of-way; and, 
 
 
WHEREAS, the above described subdivision plat was presented to the City of Cheyenne 
Planning Commission for consideration on September 5, 2023; the Planning Commission 
recommended that said plat be approved by the Governing Body; and, the plat has been duly 
executed by the Development Office. 
 
 
NOW, THEREFORE BE IT RESOLVED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE 
CITY OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING, THAT the subdivision described as R and R at 
Sweetgrass, Cheyenne, Wyoming, be and the same hereby is approved and confirmed as presented, 
and that the Mayor and the City Clerk be and are hereby authorized, empowered, and directed to 
execute said plat, when Community Facility Fees have been paid in full.

PUDC-23-100  
 
 
September 5, 2023 
Planning and Development Office 
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282   (Fax) 307-637-6366 
 
 
 
 
CITY OF CHEYENNE PLANNING COMMISSION 
SEPTEMBER 5, 2023 
STAFF REPORT 
 
FILE: 
PUDC-23-100: R+R at Sweetgrass (Expedited Review) 
REQUEST: 
A replat of all of Block 1, Sweetgrass, 2nd Filing. 
LOCATION: 
East of Sweetgrass Drive, south of College Drive 
APPLICANT: 
Casey Palma, Palma Land Planning 
OWNER: 
Sweetgrass Land CO, LLC 
ZONING: 
Sweetgrass Planned Unit Development (PUD) 
PURPOSE: 
A replat to create 10 commercial lots with open space and streets 
PREPARED BY: 
Connor White, Planner II 
 
 
  
  
  
  
  
  
  
  Planning and Development Department 
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282 
(Fax) 307-637-6366 

PUDC-23-100  
 
 
September 5, 2023 
RECOMMENDED MOTION: 
 
Move to approve the R+R at Sweetgrass Expedited Preliminary Plat, advancing the Final Plat to the 
Governing Body with staff conditions. 
 
APPLICABLE CITY CODE SECTION(S) AND PLANS: 
 
• 
UDC 2.1.2 Preliminary Plat Approval 
• 
UDC 2.1.3 Final Plat Approval  
• 
UDC Article 4 Subdivisions 
• 
UDC Article 5 Zoning Regulations 
• 
PlanCheyenne 
 
BACKGROUND: 
 
The applicant is requesting approval of a replat of 22.37 acres of land. The subject area is currently vacant. 
The proposed development is for commercial lots in the Hospitality and Events Center (HEC) section of the 
Sweetgrass Planned Unit Development (PUD).  
 
SURROUNDING LAND USE AND ZONING: 
 
 
The property is zoned as part of the Sweetgrass PUD.  The Sweetgrass PUD is generally described as “a 
±2,349.5-acre community envisioned as a distinctive mixed-use master planned village, integrating 
retail, entertainment, lodging, employment opportunities, and variety of intimately scaled residential 
neighborhoods.”  The district generally allows a broad range of uses.  
 
ANALYSIS OF APPLICATION CONFORMANCE WITH REVIEW CRITERIA: 
 
Should the Planning Commission wish to recommend approval of the Preliminary Plat, the Commission 
must make the following findings required in UDC 2.1.2.d  – a detailed analysis is below: 
 
1) The application is in accordance with the development policies and physical patterns 
identified in the Comprehensive Plan. (UDC 2.1.2.d.1) 
 
This development is in conformance with the principles and future land uses identified in 
PlanCheyenne. The property is within the Urban Service Boundary and has access to all utilities. 
The future land use map within PlanCheyenne identifies this area as developing with a mix of 
commercial and residential uses. The plat provides lots with typical commercial sizes.  
Future Land Use Designation  Zoning

PUDC-23-100  
 
 
September 5, 2023 
The application complies with this review criterion.  
 
2) The application reflects sound planning and urban design principles with respect to existing 
and potential future development in the vicinity, including consideration of current and future 
streets, open spaces, blocks and lots, and public utilities or facilities. (UDC 2.1.2.d.2) 
 
The plat is in the Sweetgrass PUD which sets forth alternative standards for blocks and open 
space. The plat meshes well with the Sweetgrass development scheme. Sufficient space and 
corridors are provided for public access and facilities. The block network represents a sound 
urban design principle of interconnected, smaller blocks. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
   
3) The division of land meets the purposes, intent, and all applicable standards in Article 4. (UDC 
2.1.2.d.3) 
 
Community Facility Fees (4.2): 
For fees based on acreage, the area of the plat is pro-rated to the nearest tenth of an acre. 
Fees previously paid may be credited toward the payment currently due. This land was 
previously platted in 2019, and plat fees have not charged since that time.  
Total Fees due at signing of the plat: 
 
$0.00 
 
Per UDC Section 4.2.5, Table 4-2, Enhancement Fees are payable at the issuance of a 
building permit for each structure. 
Commercial Enhancement Fee 
$1,100 per acre 
 
 
 
Transportation Network (4.3): 
This is a replat of an area that has previously been approved. The plat proposes re-arranging 
some streets, but does not substantively change the connections made. The street named 
‘Prairie Gold Lane’ has right-of-way width to be a major street, but has curves too tight for 
typical major street design speeds; however, due to the short length and low levels of 
anticipated traffic, the City Engineer’s Office is willing to consider a lower speed for road 
design. The right-of-way centerline is not quite aligned appropriately for this lower design 
speed and adjustments are required accordingly. 
 
Open Space (4.4): 
Open space parcels is provided on this plat. There are also open space easements proposed. 
These tracks and easements are part of Sweetgrass PUD trail system. 
 
Required Improvements (4.5): 
This plat appears to be able to provide all required utilities to the lots within this 
development. 
 
Public Safety Fees (4.6): 
Public Safety Fees are required at the time of construction of any commercial building on 
any of the proposed lots. Per UDC Section 4.6.3.b, Public Safety Fees are payable at the 
issuance of a building permit for each structure.  
 
The application complies with this review criterion, with conditions.   
 

PUDC-23-100  
 
 
September 5, 2023 
4) Any phasing proposed in the application is clearly indicated and demonstrates a logical and 
well-planned development pattern. This may include consideration of amenities and public 
facilities of the subdivision within each phase compared to the anticipated timing or the 
likelihood of future phases being constructed as planned. (UDC 2.1.2.d.4) 
 
No specific phasing is proposed with this project. 
 
Review criterion not applicable.   
 
5) The impacts identified in specific studies or technical reports that were prepared or requested 
to be prepared in association with the application, and whether the findings and 
recommendations of these reports, or any modifications or conditions on approval of the 
application, can mitigate these impacts. (UDC 2.1.2.d.5) 
 
Drainage: 
A Final Stormwater Management Plan for Phase 1 of Sweetgrass was submitted and reviewed. 
There were no concerns with drainage at the plat level for the project as presented.   
 
Traffic: 
A Transportation Impact Study was submitted and reviewed. There were no concerns with 
traffic at the plat level for the project as presented. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion. 
  
6) Lots proposed for development shall be buildable lots according to the existing or proposed 
zoning district standards, result in design and development patterns that meet the Intent and 
Applicability statements of the existing or proposed zoning district, and not aggravate 
nonconforming setbacks or currently adopted building and fire code access and separation 
requirements. (UDC 2.1.2.d.6) 
 
This plat is for the Hospitality Center (HEC) section of the Sweetgrass PUD (on both the current 
PUD and the proposed PUD amendment). This section of the PUD is anticipated to develop with 
hospitality-centered businesses (such as hotels, restaurants and conference centers). The lots 
and blocks being provided with this plat represent a more diffuse, multiple-business model for 
the Hospitality Center – instead of a large, single-entity site that the previous plat anticipated. 
 
The proposed plat changes the street layout by shifting the N-S street and adding a central E-W 
street. From a connectivity standpoint, this plat provides more connections and smaller blocks 
than the previous plat – although this does mean it would be more difficult for a large, single-
entity hospitality center to develop.    
 
All lots proposed for development are buildable lots in this zone district. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
 
7) All parcels not proposed as buildable lots and which are not dedicated to and accepted by the 
City shall have a clearly defined entity responsible for ongoing maintenance.  (UDC 2.1.2.d.7) 
 

PUDC-23-100  
 
 
September 5, 2023 
The PUD requires common lots to be owned, developed, improved, managed, and/or 
maintained by homeowners’ associations, architectural review board(s), community 
development districts, and/or similar entities (PUD Section 1, Subsection G). 
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
 
8) The application will not create any undue influence on existing or potential development for 
adjacent property considering the existing zoning and Comprehensive Plan policies for that 
property.  (UDC 2.1.2.d.8) 
 
This plat is in conformance with the design and layout anticipated for this parcel by the 
Sweetgrass PUD. Adjacent Sweetgrass developments were designed and laid out with this 
development as an integral part of the broader development pattern.  
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
 
9) The application is not harmful to the public health, safety, and welfare. (UDC 2.1.2.d.9) 
 
City staff is satisfied that this proposed plat would not adversely affect the public health, safety, 
or general welfare. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
 
10) Remainder lot(s) shall be clearly identified on the preliminary plat and shall not be developed 
or proposed for development for purposes.  Remainder lot(s) shall be noted and described on 
all plats either graphically or by legal description. (UDC 2.1.2.d.10) 
 
Remainder lots are not proposed with this plat. 
 
Review criterion not applicable. 
 
11) The layout and design of the final plat is in substantial compliance with the approved 
preliminary plat considering the number of lots or parcels, street and block layout, and access, 
and any deviations in the final plat brings the application in further compliance with the 
Comprehensive Plan. (UDC 2.1.3.d.1) 
 
The Final Plat submitted with the application is substantially similar to the Preliminary Plat 
submitted. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
 
12) The application meets any conditions or modifications of the approved preliminary plat. (UDC 
2.1.3.d.2) 
 
Through the review process and application of the Preliminary Plat, Staff recommends minor 
corrections as conditions to the plat. These corrections should be resolved prior to final plat 
approval.  
 
The application complies with this review criterion, with conditions.   
   

PUDC-23-100  
 
 
September 5, 2023 
13) The application meets the purposes, intent, and applicable standards of Article 4. (UDC 
2.1.3.d.3) 
 
As a concurrent Preliminary and Final Plat application, this Final Plat review criterion is covered 
by the equivalent Preliminary Plat review criterion (subsection 3 above). 
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
 
CONFORMANCE WITH APPLICABLE PLANS: 
The project is located in an area covered by the following plans: 
• 
PlanCheyenne 
Conformance with PlanCheyenne is covered by review criterion 1. 
 
STAFF REVIEW: 
 
Through the review process, comments were received regarding the following issues: 
1. Minor clerical corrections (partially corrected with revised submittal). 
2. Design speed of Prairie Gold Lane 
3. Lot size applicability to the Hospitality and Events Center zone of the PUD. 
 
CONCLUSION: 
 
Staff analysis is that the application meets the review criteria and therefore recommends approval of 
the expedited plat application as submitted by the applicant.  
 
The area this plat is proposed in is anticipated to develop with a commercial hospitality center, based on 
both the existing zoning and the patterns proposed by the comprehensive plan. The proposed plat is 
consistent with these desired development patterns.   
   
ALTERNATIVES: 
 
1. Recommend approval of the expedited preliminary plat, forwarding the Final Plat to the 
Governing Body. 
2. Recommend approval of the preliminary plat with conditions and that a final plat addressing the 
conditions of approval be prepared and presented to the Governing Body.  
3. Recommend approval of the preliminary plat as submitted by the applicant and forward the 
preliminary plat to the Governing Body. 
4. Recommend approval of the preliminary plat subject to staff’s recommended conditions and 
forward the preliminary plat to the Governing Body.  
5. Recommend denial of the preliminary plat based on findings of denial. The Planning Commission 
must conclude that the project does not meet all the review criteria. As part of the motion, 
findings for denial must be stated. 
6. Postpone the project until issues identified during the meeting can be resolved. 
 
General Information Regarding Alternatives: 
The Planning Commission may modify staff’s recommended conditions, delete conditions, add 
conditions, or decide contrary to staff’s recommendation. In the event conditions are modified, the 
conditions shall relate to applicable review criteria. In the event that the Commission acts contrary to 
staff’s recommendation or modifies conditions of approval, the Commission shall cite specific reasons 
(based on the review criteria) for approval or denial. 

PUDC-23-100  
 
 
September 5, 2023 
STAFF RECOMMENDATION: 
 
Staff recommends approval of the expedited Preliminary Plat, advancing the Final Plat to the Governing 
Body with the following conditions: 
1. Prior to Governing Body approval: 
a. Modify the curves on the Prairie Gold Lane right-of-way so the centerline meets 
AASHTO standards for a minimum 30mph design speed. 
b. Number the blocks, and renumber or rename the lots appropriately. 
 
ATTACHMENTS: 
1. Zoning Map  
2. Land Use Plan Map 
3. Master Application Form 
4. Preliminary Plat 
5. Final Plat

MEMO 
To: 
Honorable Mayor Patrick Collins and Members of the City Council 
From: 
Lonnie Olson, Planner I 
Subject: PUDC-23-102 / Hitching Post Plaza, 3rd Filing / Final Plat  
Date: 
September 11, 2023 
 
Background:  
The item for consideration is a Final Plat. This is approved by Resolution.    
 
The proposed Governing Body review schedule is as follows: 
• 
Introduction and Referral 9/11/2023 
• 
Public Services Committee 9/19/2023 
• 
Final consideration 9/25/2023 
 
Planning Commission Recommendation:  
The Planning Commission heard this item at their September 5, 2023 meeting and unanimously voted to 
recommend the Governing Body to approve this application, with staff’s recommended condition.  
 
A revised plat was submitted by the applicant but was not submitted in time for Staff to review prior to 
the creation of the Planning Commission Staff Report. Staff review of the revised plat found it addressed 
all of the Staff conditions in the Planning Commission Staff Report. Upon Staffs review of the revised plat 
a new condition was added. The condition is: 
“ 1. Prior to Governing Body approval: 
a. Revise storm sewer easement to the satisfaction of the City Engineer.”    
  
The Planning Commission Staff Report is attached and remains unchanged.

CITY OF CHEYENNE PLANNING COMMISSION 
September 5, 2023 
STAFF REPORT 
 
FILE: 
PUDC-23-102: Hitching Post Plaza, 3rd Filing (Expedited Review) 
REQUEST: 
A plat creating eight lots for commercial development and possibly multifamily 
residential. 
LOCATION: 
1600 W Lincolnway 
APPLICANT: 
Casey Palma, Palma Land Planning LLC 
OWNER: 
1600 Hitching Post, LLC 
ZONING: 
CB Community Business  
PURPOSE: 
A plat subdividing one lot into eight lots to allow for commercial 
development and possibly multifamily. 
PREPARED BY: 
Lonnie Olson, Planner I 
 
  
  
  
  
  
  
  
  Planning and Development Department 
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282 
(Fax) 307-637-6366 

PUDC-23-102  
 
 
September 5, 2023 
 
RECOMMENDED MOTION: 
 
Move to approve the Hitching Post, 3rd Filing Expedited Preliminary Plat, advancing the Final Plat to the 
Governing Body, with conditions listed in the staff report. 
 
APPLICABLE CITY CODE SECTION(S) AND PLANS: 
 
• 
UDC 2.1.2 Preliminary Plat Approval 
• 
UDC 2.1.3 Final Plat Approval  
• 
UDC Article 4 Subdivisions 
• 
UDC Article 5 Zoning Regulations 
• 
PlanCheyenne 
 
BACKGROUND: 
 
The applicant is requesting approval of a plat for 5.41 acres of land. The subject property is currently vacant. 
The proposed development is commercial development and possibly multifamily residential. There is a 
concurrent zone change for this property, PUDC-23-103, which proposes changing the zone of most of the 
property from CB Community Business to MUB Mixed-Use Business. 
 
Staff received a revised submittal which may address all concerns; however the review is not complete at the 
time of this staff report.  
 
SURROUNDING LAND USE AND ZONING: 
 
 
ANALYSIS OF APPLICATION CONFORMANCE WITH REVIEW CRITERIA: 
 
Should the Planning Commission wish to recommend approval of the Preliminary Plat, the Commission 
must make the following findings required in UDC 2.1.2.d  – a detailed analysis is below: 
 
1) The application is in accordance with the development policies and physical patterns 
identified in the Comprehensive Plan. (UDC 2.1.2.d.1) 
 
This development is in conformance with the principles and future land uses identified in 
PlanCheyenne. The property is within the Urban Service Boundary and has access to all utilities. 
Future Land Use Designation  Zoning

PUDC-23-102  
 
 
September 5, 2023 
The future land use map within PlanCheyenne identifies this area as developing with mixed-use 
commercial and mixed-use residential. PlanCheyenne encourages a mix of commercial and 
residential types.  
 
The application complies with this review criterion.  
 
2) The application reflects sound planning and urban design principles with respect to existing 
and potential future development in the vicinity, including consideration of current and future 
streets, open spaces, blocks and lots, and public utilities or facilities. (UDC 2.1.2.d.2) 
 
The proposed plat is a replat of a single lot which is within an existing subdivision. The location 
of streets, blocks, and public facilities are not being modified with this plat. The plat proposes 
internal access through easements rather than through streets. The subdivision would create 8 
lots which would allow for commercial development and possibly multifamily.  
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
   
3) The division of land meets the purposes, intent, and all applicable standards in Article 4. (UDC 
2.1.2.d.3) 
 
Community Facility Fees (4.2): 
For fees based on acreage, the area of the plat is pro-rated to the nearest tenth of an acre. The 
required fees for the 3.4 acres of non-exempt land in this plat are as follows: 
Land Acquisition Fee  
 
 
 
$1,190 ($350 per acre) 
Infrastructure Fee  
 
 
 
$1,190 ($350 per acre) 
Total Land Acquisition & Infrastructure Fees 
$2,380.00 
Per UDC Section 4.2.5, Table 4-2, the Land Acquisition & Infrastructure fee are payable at the 
time of the signing of the final plat. Park fees previously paid are credited to those currently 
due. Park fees were previously paid for by a platting action in 2023 and park fees have not 
changed since that time. No park fees will be charged at the signing of this plat.   
 
Residential Enhancement Fee 
$400 per dwelling unit 
Commercial Enhancement Fee $1,100 per acre 
Per UDC Section 4.2.5, Table 4-2, Enhancement Fees are payable at the issuance of a building 
permit for each structure. 
 
 
Transportation Network (4.3): 
This plat is occurring within an area of the community which has been previously developed. 
The existing block and street network largely meet applicable standards.  
 
Open Space (4.4): 
Open space is required at 750sqft per unit or 8% of the gross development area, whichever 
is less. However, due to the size of the property, plat-level open space is not required. 
  
Required Improvements (4.5): 
This plat appears to be able to provide all required utilities to the lots within this 
development. 
 
Public Safety Fees (4.6): 

PUDC-23-102  
 
 
September 5, 2023 
Public Safety Fees are required at $603.42 per 1000 square feet of finished floor area. Public 
Safety Fees are required at $949.37 per dwelling unit. Per UDC Section 4.6.3.b, Public Safety 
Fees are payable at the issuance of a building permit for each structure.  
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
 
4) Any phasing proposed in the application is clearly indicated and demonstrates a logical and 
well-planned development pattern. This may include consideration of amenities and public 
facilities of the subdivision within each phase compared to the anticipated timing or the 
likelihood of future phases being constructed as planned. (UDC 2.1.2.d.4) 
 
No further phasing is proposed with this project. 
 
Review criterion not applicable.   
 
5) The impacts identified in specific studies or technical reports that were prepared or requested 
to be prepared in association with the application, and whether the findings and 
recommendations of these reports, or any modifications or conditions on approval of the 
application, can mitigate these impacts. (UDC 2.1.2.d.5) 
 
Drainage: 
The plat provides drainage easements and a drainage area. The applicant is working with the 
City Engineer on Engineering Plan Review and drainage concerns.  
 
Traffic: 
The previous Traffic Worksheet was submitted and reviewed. There were concerns with traffic 
at the plat level based on trip generation.   
 
The application complies with this review criterion, with conditions. 
  
6) Lots proposed for development shall be buildable lots according to the existing or proposed 
zoning district standards, result in design and development patterns that meet the Intent and 
Applicability statements of the existing or proposed zoning district, and not aggravate 
nonconforming setbacks or currently adopted building and fire code access and separation 
requirements. (UDC 2.1.2.d.6) 
 
The proposed plat proposes eight lots. Lot four and lot two are not proposed as buildable lots. 
Lot four is proposed to be a lot for drainage and lot 2 is proposed to be an additional shared 
access and parking. The lots are intended to be buildable lots in the proposed zone district.  
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
 
7) All parcels not proposed as buildable lots and which are not dedicated to and accepted by the 
City shall have a clearly defined entity responsible for ongoing maintenance.  (UDC 2.1.2.d.7) 
 
The plat proposes multiple lots that are not considered buildable. These lots contain drainage 
and access easements. With the original submittal of the plat no entity was identified to 
maintain these lots.   
 

PUDC-23-102  
 
 
September 5, 2023 
The application does not comply with this review criterion, however this can be fulfilled with 
conditions.   
 
8) The application will not create any undue influence on existing or potential development for 
adjacent property considering the existing zoning and Comprehensive Plan policies for that 
property.  (UDC 2.1.2.d.8) 
 
Properties adjacent to this plat are zoned MUB, CB and LI. Some surrounding parcels are already 
developed. The proposed plat will not prohibit development on any of the adjacent parcels. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
 
9) The application is not harmful to the public health, safety, and welfare. (UDC 2.1.2.d.9) 
 
City staff is satisfied that this proposed plat would not adversely affect the public health, safety, 
or general welfare. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
 
10) Remainder lot(s) shall be clearly identified on the preliminary plat and shall not be developed 
or proposed for development for purposes.  Remainder lot(s) shall be noted and described on 
all plats either graphically or by legal description. (UDC 2.1.2.d.10) 
 
Remainder lots are not proposed with this plat. 
 
Review criterion not applicable. 
 
11) The layout and design of the final plat is in substantial compliance with the approved 
preliminary plat considering the number of lots or parcels, street and block layout, and access, 
and any deviations in the final plat brings the application in further compliance with the 
Comprehensive Plan. (UDC 2.1.3.d.1) 
 
The Final Plat submitted with the application is substantially similar to the Preliminary Plat 
submitted. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
 
12) The application meets any conditions or modifications of the approved preliminary plat. (UDC 
2.1.3.d.2) 
 
Through the review process and application of the Preliminary Plat, Staff does recommend 
conditions to the plat. All conditions are anticipated to be addressed prior to final approval.  
 
The application complies with this review criterion, with conditions.   
   
13) The application meets the purposes, intent, and applicable standards of Article 4. (UDC 
2.1.3.d.3) 
 
As a concurrent Preliminary and Final Plat application, this Final Plat review criterion is covered 
by the equivalent Preliminary Plat review criterion (subsection 3 above). 

PUDC-23-102  
 
 
September 5, 2023 
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
 
CONFORMANCE WITH APPLICABLE PLANS: 
The project is located in an area covered by the following plans: 
1. PlanCheyenne 
Conformance with PlanCheyenne is covered by review criterion 1. 
 
STAFF REVIEW: 
 
Through the review process, comments were received regarding the following issues: 
1. Minor clerical corrections  
2. Concerns regarding drainage  
3. Concerns regarding the new lots and building sizes in relation to the trip generation 
assignments  
4. Acreage to calculate park fees 
 
The applicant has submitted revisions which may address all above comments.  
 
CONCLUSION: 
 
Staff analysis is that the application meets the review criteria and therefore recommends approval of 
the expedited plat application as submitted by the applicant, with conditions.  
 
The area this plat is proposed in is anticipated to develop as a largely commercial development with 
some possibilities of residential development, based on both existing development and the patterns 
proposed by the comprehensive plan. The proposed plat is consistent with this development pattern.   
   
ALTERNATIVES: 
 
1. Recommend approval of the expedited preliminary plat, forwarding the Final Plat to the 
Governing Body. 
2. Recommend approval of the preliminary plat with conditions and that a final plat addressing the 
conditions of approval be prepared and presented to the Governing Body.  
3. Recommend approval of the preliminary plat as submitted by the applicant and forward the 
preliminary plat to the Governing Body. 
4. Recommend approval of the preliminary plat subject to staff’s recommended conditions and 
forward the preliminary plat to the Governing Body.  
5. Recommend denial of the preliminary plat based on findings of denial. The Planning Commission 
must conclude that the project does not meet all the review criteria. As part of the motion, 
findings for denial must be stated. 
6. Postpone the project until issues identified during the meeting can be resolved. 
 
General Information Regarding Alternatives: 
The Planning Commission may modify staff’s recommended conditions, delete conditions, add 
conditions, or decide contrary to staff’s recommendation. In the event conditions are modified, the 
conditions shall relate to applicable review criteria. In the event that the Commission acts contrary to 
staff’s recommendation or modifies conditions of approval, the Commission shall cite specific reasons 
(based on the review criteria) for approval or denial. 
 

PUDC-23-102  
 
 
September 5, 2023 
STAFF RECOMMENDATION: 
 
Staff recommends approval of the expedited Preliminary Plat, advancing the Final Plat to the Governing 
Body with the following conditions:  
1. Prior to Governing Body approval fulfill the following: 
a. Update the Traffic Impact Study for new trip assignments based on this plat 
b. Make clerical corrections  
c. Provide a defined owner maintenance agency for lot 4  
d. Provide an Engineering Plan Review (or alternative documentation satisfactory to the 
City Engineer) 
 
Should the applicant submit revisions which are determined to fulfill these conditions prior to the 
Planning Commission meeting, Staff may recommend approval without some or all of the above 
conditions.   
 
ATTACHMENTS: 
1. Zoning Map  
2. Land Use Plan Map 
3. Master Application Form 
4. Preliminary Plat 
5. Final Plat

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2023-09-05_TotalExtraction_36466372.pdf
 
RESOLUTION NO. __________ 
 
ENTITLED: "A RESOLUTION EXPRESSING THE SUPPORT OF THE GOVERNING 
BODY FOR THE DESIGNATION OF THE HIGH PLAINS ARBORETUM 
AS A STATE HISTORIC SITE BY THE WYOMING STATE 
LEGISLATURE." 
 
WHEREAS, when the City of Cheyenne was established on August 8, 1867, only twelve 
(12) trees were located within city limits, and as early as 1882, Cheyenne residents began playing 
an important role in increasing the tree canopy within the city; 
 
WHEREAS, since 1928 the City of Cheyenne has owned two thousand one hundred forty 
(2,140) acres of property west of F.E. Warren Air Force Base known as the High Plains Arboretum 
(the "Arboretum"). Beginning in 1928, the City leased the property to the U.S. Department of 
Agriculture ("USDA") for one hundred ninety-nine (199) years for the purpose of operating the 
USDA Cheyenne Horticultural Field Station (the "USDA Station"); 
 
WHEREAS, as part of President Franklin D. Roosevelt's New Deal legislation, members 
of the Civilian Conservation Corps ("CCC") constructed important infrastructure and several 
historic structures at the Arboretum. These structures include the water treatment plant resources 
on Round Top, much of the footprint of the original horticultural research station, including the 
headhouse, greenhouse and foundations, the lath house, as well as the irrigation infrastructure, 
parade ground, the former USDA soils lab and historic planting grounds. Notably, the Arboretum 
also includes Houses 1 through 4, which were all designed by prolific Cheyenne architect, and 
former member of the Wyoming Legislature, William DuBois; 
 
WHEREAS, the USDA Station researched hardy trees, shrubs, fruits and vegetables that 
could survive the harsh Wyoming weather and climate with the intent of propagating these plants 
for the people of Wyoming.  
 
WHEREAS, the mission of the USDA Station changed in 1974 from horticultural research 
to livestock grazing management, mined land reclamation and water conservation research. The 
change in mission led to the abandonment of historic tree property encompassing approximately 
sixty-two (62) acres, which was minimally cared for by the USDA staff from 1974 until the early 
2000s when the City of Cheyenne became involved with its care and upkeep; 
 
WHEREAS, as the grasslands research became the focus of the USDA, care for the trees 
and shrubs was curtailed, leaving them to rely mainly on natural precipitation to survive. Many 
trees perished as a result, with over fifty percent (50%) of the plants inventoried in 1974 now gone, 
and many more that are in severe decline; 
 
WHEREAS, Cheyenne has experienced high mortality rates in its street tree populations 
resulting from an aging forest and past extreme weather events, which has caused large gaps in the 
tree canopy. Recognizing the need for a community tree planting program, the City of Cheyenne 
Division of Urban Forestry created Rooted in Cheyenne, a non-profit organization staffed by 
2:59 pm, Sep 06 2023

Page 2 of 3 
volunteers who plant trees throughout the city. Since its creation, Rooted in Cheyenne has planted 
over one thousand two hundred (1,200) trees in the city; 
 
 
WHEREAS, the City has also partnered with the Friends of the Botanic Gardens 
Foundation, which is a non-profit organization that beautifies and enriches the High Plains through 
gardening, volunteerism, education and stewardship. The work of the of foundation includes the 
support of the Arboretum; 
 
WHEREAS, the cost of maintaining the Arboretum and appurtenant ground buildings, and 
ensuring the continued existence of the trees and shrubs, present-day grounds and historic CCC 
structures are a significant financial challenge for the City of Cheyenne; 
 
WHEREAS, given the critical historical and cultural importance of the Arboretum and the 
additional support this site requires, on May 1, 2023, Cheyenne Mayor Patrick Collins formally 
requested that the Wyoming Department of State Parks and Cultural Resources (the 
"Department"), review the Arboretum as a potential historic site through its Site Criteria Process, 
The Department has convened a working group to evaluate the potential to administer the 
Arboretum as a historic site; 
 
WHEREAS, based on conversations with multiple vested stakeholders, the Department 
believes that to best operate the Arboretum as a State Historic Site in collaboration with these 
community partners; 
 
WHEREAS, the Department has proposed state historic site boundary encompassing eight 
hundred seventy seven (877) acres of the 2,149-acre property. This acreage would ensure that the 
Department and vested stakeholders could work to preserve and improve the buildings and other 
structures on site as well as maintain the appurtenant features, including roads, irrigation ditches 
and fencing; and, 
 
WHEREAS, the Joint Travel, Recreation, Wildlife and Cultural Resources Interim 
Committee of the Wyoming State Legislature is currently considering legislation that if introduced 
and passed during the 2024 Budget Session would designate the Arboretum as a State Historic 
Site. 
 
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY 
OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING, hereby expresses its strong support for the Joint Travel, 
Recreation, Wildlife & Cultural Resources Interim Committee's sponsorship and introduction of 
State Historic Site designating the Arboretum as a state historic site and for the adoption of this 
important legislation by the Wyoming State Legislature during the 2024 Budget Session. 
 
 
 
 
 
 

Page 3 of 3 
PRESENTED, READ AND ADOPTED this _______ day of _____________________, 
2023. 
 
 
__________________________________________  
Patrick Collins, Mayor  
 
 
(SEAL)

Kristina F. Jones, City Clerk

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2023-09-05_TotalExtraction_30330472.pdf
Planning and Development Office 
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282   
(Fax) 307-637-6366 
 
 
 
 
MEMO 
To: 
Honorable Mayor Patrick Collins and Members of the City Council 
From: 
Charles W. Bloom, AICP 
Subject: County Pocket Annexation #2   
Date: 
September 25, 2023 
 
Background:  
 
The item for consideration is a resolution to allow the City to proceed with a City-initiated annexation. 
Annexation of County pockets is a 2022 City Council priority.  A “County pocket” is an area of 
unincorporated land 100% surrounded by the City of Cheyenne.   
 
The proposed Governing Body review schedule is as follows: 
• 
Introduction and referral  
September 25, 2023 
• 
Public Services Committee  
October 3, 2023 
• 
Final consideration 
 
October 9, 2023 
 
This annexation is comprised of 50 properties 100% surrounded by the City (See attached map). Of 
the 50 parcels, 26 are developed with residential uses, one is developed with a commercial use, and 
others are undeveloped or incorporated into adjacent parcels under similar ownership. The City has 
maintained contact with the County Planning Office to coordinate any pending permits or 
development reviews.  Currently there are no active permits or development specific to the 
properties proposed for annexation.   
 
On September 20, 2023, City staff mailed a courtesy notice by first class mail to all landowners 
included in this annexation area inviting them to a “Question and Answer” on Tuesday, October 3, 
2023.  A media release was also released on September 22, 2023, identifying properties included in 
this Count pocket annexation. 
 
Annexation also requires establishing a City zone. Staff has created a tentative timeline for the 
annexation and establishment of City zoning districts for the subject properties. Zone districts will be 
established on the assigned zoning process of Unified Development Code (UDC) section 2.2.8. In the 
event of split zoning (where two zone districts divide an existing tract of land), the City intends to 
apply the more restrictive zone unless extenuating circumstances exist.

WHEREAS, Wyoming Statute § 15-1-404 specifies the requirements for initiating 
annexation proceedings by cities and towns and requires that a resolution be adopted certifying 
compliance with Wyoming Statute § 15-1-402;  
 
WHEREAS, based on reasonable evidence, the Governing Body has determined that the 
territory to be annexed complies with Wyoming Statute § 15-1-402 and meets the following 
criteria found in Wyoming Statute § 15-1-402(a):  
 
(i) The annexation of the area is for the protection of the health, safety and welfare of the 
persons residing in the area and in the City of Cheyenne;  
 
(ii) The urban development of the area sought to be annexed would constitute a natural, 
geographical, economical and social part of the City of Cheyenne;  
 
(iii) The areas sought to be annexed is a logical and feasible addition to the City of 
Cheyenne and the extension of basic and other services customarily available to residents 
of the city or town shall, within reason, be available to the area proposed to be annexed;  
 
(iv) The area sought to be annexed is contiguous with or adjacent to the City of Cheyenne;  
 
(v)  The Governing Body is prepared to issue one (1) or more franchises as necessary to 
serve the annexed area pursuant to Wyoming Statute § 15-1-410; and, 
 
(vi)  The City of Cheyenne is prepared to, not less than twenty (20) business days prior to 
the public hearing required by Wyoming Statute § 15-1-405(a), send by certified mail to 
all landowners and affected public utilities within the territory a summary of the annexation 
report as required under Wyoming Statute § 15-1-402(c) and notice of the time, date and 
location of the public hearing required by Wyoming Statute § 15-1-405(a).  
 
WHEREAS, an annexation report has been created by the City of Cheyenne, containing, 
at a minimum, the required information from Wyo. Stat. § 15-1-402(c); 
 
WHEREAS, The City of Cheyenne shall prepare for each landowner and affected public 
utility so requesting in writing, the estimated cost of infrastructure improvements required of the 
landowner and affected public utility related to the annexation pursuant to Wyo. Stat. § 15-1-
402(e); 
 

Page 8 of 9 
 
WHEREAS, the annexation of this territory will not create an area which is situated entirely 
within the boundaries of the City of Cheyenne, following criteria found in Wyoming Statute § 15 
1-406(d); and 
 
WHEREAS, the Governing Body finds the territory to be annexed does not meet the 
criteria specified under Wyoming Statute § 15 1 412, and thus does not require the written consent 
of the owner of the territory before being annexed by the City of Cheyenne.  
 
 
NOW, THEREFORE BE IT RESOLVED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY 
OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING, THAT the land described above is compliant with Wyo. Stat. §15-
1-402, and that the procedures outlined in Wyo. Stat. § 15-1-405 and Wyo.  
Stat. § 15-1-406 shall hereby be initiated. 
 
PRESENTED, READ AND ADOPTED this _______ day of _____________________, 
2023.

resolution is necessary to allow the City to proceed with a City-initiated annexation.

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2023-09-05_TotalExtraction_72979046.pdf
RESOLUTION NO. __________ 
 
ENTITLED: "A RESOLUTION EXPRESSING THE WILL OF THE GOVERNING 
BODY FOR THE TEMPORARY INSTALLATION OF ADDITIONAL 
TRAFFIC CONTROL DEVICES AT SPECIFIED LOCATIONS ALONG 
GARDENIA DRIVE TO COMBAT THE SERIOUS PROBLEM OF 
SPEEDING VEHICLES." 
 
WHEREAS, Gardenia Drive is a collector road that is located in the northern part of 
Cheyenne, which begins on Yellowstone Road and travels east through the neighborhoods of 
Monterey Heights, Bel Air Hills and The Pointe, and terminates at Powderhouse Road; 
 
WHEREAS, as a collector road, Gardenia Drive was designed for low-to-moderate traffic 
capacity and to move vehicles at lower speeds between local streets and arterial roads such as 
Interstate 25;  
 
WHEREAS, as a collector road, Gardenia Drive has a posted speed limit of thirty (30) 
miler per hour along the entirety of its route; 
 
WHEREAS, over at least the prior ten (10) years, residents of the neighborhoods adjacent 
to Gardenia Drive have experienced an increasing amount of traffic, which has in turn resulted in 
an increased amount of people disregarding the posted speed limit and rushing through these 
neighborhoods in their cars and trucks; 
 
WHEREAS, these speeding vehicles unreasonably disturb the quietude of this area and 
place the residents of these neighborhoods at an increased risk of physical danger; 
 
WHEREAS, the impacts from these speeding vehicles present a serious problem, which 
does not appear to be one that will disappear without affirmative action by the City of Cheyenne; 
 
 WHEREAS, in pursuit of a permanent solution, the Governing Body intends to implement 
a temporary fix to address this situation through the increased use of certain traffic control devices; 
and 
 
WHEREAS, the Governing Body finds that the most appropriate traffic control devices to 
implement at this time are stop signs at designated intersections along Gardenia Drive. 
 
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY 
OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING, that stop signs shall be installed at the intersections of: (1) Bomar 
Drive and Gardenia Drive; (2) Pasadena Road and Gardenia Drive; and (3) Legacy Parkway and 
Gardenia Drive; and 
 
 
 
9:09 am, Sep 20, 2023

Page 2 of 2 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED, that the stop signs required in accordance with this 
resolution shall be installed in a manner to ensure that the vehicles passing through these 
designated intersections will all be required to stop regardless of the vehicle's route of travel.  
 
PRESENTED, READ AND ADOPTED this _______ day of _____________________, 
2023. 
 
 
__________________________________________  
Patrick Collins, Mayor  
 
 
(SEAL)  
 
 
ATTEST: 
 
____________________________________  
Kristina F. Jones, City Clerk 


--- END OF FILE ---


# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2023-09-05_TotalExtraction_36909541.pdf
RESOLUTION NO. __________ 
 
ENTITLED: "A RESOLUTION REQUIRING THE STUDY AND SUBMITTAL OF A 
REPORT TO THE GOVERNING BODY REGARDING VEHICLES 
VIOLATING POSTED SPEED LIMITS ON THE CITY'S ROADWAYS 
AND VEHICLES DISREGARDING TRAFFIC SIGNALS AND RUNNING 
RED LIGHTS." 
 
WHEREAS, according to the 2020 Census from the U.S. Census Bureau, the population 
of the city of Cheyenne is 65,132, making it the most populated city in Wyoming; 
 
WHEREAS, being the most populated city, Cheyenne has an increased amount of vehicle 
traffic and congestion, which in turn, causes an increased amount of traffic enforcement issues; 
 
WHEREAS, these traffic enforcement issues vary but of particular concern are issues 
relating to vehicles violating posted speed limits on the city's roadways and vehicles disregarding 
traffic signals and running red lights; 
 
WHEREAS, speeding vehicles in Cheyenne can create inordinate noise and significantly 
endanger the people of Cheyenne; 
 
WHEREAS, vehicles disregarding traffic signals and running red lights also significantly 
endanger the people of Cheyenne; 
 
WHEREAS, according to the Insurance Institute for Highway Safety, there were 12,330 
speeding-related fatalities in the United States in 2021, with speeding-related fatalities accounting 
for twenty-nine percent (29%) of traffic deaths that year; 
 
WHEREAS, according to the Governors Highway Safety Association, an estimated 
116,000 people were injured and 928 people were killed in crashes that involved red light running 
in the United States for the year 2020—half of those killed were pedestrians, bicyclists and people 
in other vehicles who were hit by the red light runners; 
 
WHEREAS, under Wyoming Statute § 15-1-103(a)(xi), cities and towns have the authority 
to "[t]ake all necessary action to plan, construct or otherwise improve, modify, repair, maintain 
and regulate the use of streets[.]" 
 
WHEREAS, under Section 10.12.130 of the city code, the city traffic engineer, under the 
supervision and direction of the city engineer, is responsible for the installation and maintenance 
of traffic control devices, and for maintaining the plan for the operation of traffic on the streets 
and highways of the city; 
 
WHEREAS, in accordance with the authority vested by state statute and under the city 
code, the Cheyenne Police Department is responsible for enforcing the traffic laws within 
Cheyenne; 
 
9:21 am, Sep 20, 2023

Page 2 of 2 
WHEREAS, due to the size of Cheyenne, staffing challenges and other logistical 
constraints, the Cheyenne Police Department simply cannot be everywhere at all times to monitor 
and apprehend those who choose to violate posted speed limits or run red lights;  
 
WHEREAS, because of the problems and dangers presented by this illegal conduct, the 
Governing Body believes it is necessary for the Office of City Engineer in conjunction with the 
Cheyenne Police Department to identify, study and analyze those areas within Cheyenne where 
occurrences of unlawfully speeding vehicles and vehicles running red lights are most acute and 
serious; 
 
 
WHEREAS, based on their analysis and relying upon each agency's individual expertise, 
the Governing Body seeks that a report be submitted articulating the analysis conducted and any 
recommendations the agencies may have for better addressing and enhancing the enforcement of 
the city's traffic laws pertaining to speeding vehicles and vehicles running red lights, which 
recommendations may include implementing additional traffic control devices or the installation 
and use of automated enforcement systems such as speed and red light cameras; and 
 
WHEREAS, if the recommendations include the installation and use of automated 
enforcement systems, an identification of the most appropriate devices and most qualified vendors 
and service providers are to be included in the report, along with estimates on any associated costs. 
 
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY 
OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING, that the Office of City Engineer in conjunction with the Cheyenne 
Police Department shall conduct the study and analysis and submit the report to the Governing 
Body on or before December 31, 2023, detailing the study's results and any recommendations 
consistent with the provisions of this resolution; and 
 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED, that upon submittal of the report, the Governing Body 
shall schedule a work session during which appropriate staff from the Office of City Engineer and 
the Cheyenne Police Department shall present the results of the study, the substance of the report 
and associated recommendations for better addressing and enhancing the enforcement of the city's 
traffic laws relative to unlawfully speeding vehicles and vehicles running red lights. 
 
PRESENTED, READ AND ADOPTED this _______ day of _____________________, 
2023. 
 
__________________________________________  
Patrick Collins, Mayor  
 
(SEAL)  
 
 
ATTEST: 
 
____________________________________  
Kristina F. Jones, City Clerk 


--- END OF FILE ---

CITY CONTRACT NO. __________ 
 
 
This Agreement is entered into this _______ day of ____________________, 2023, by and 
between the City of Cheyenne, a municipal corporation organized under the laws of the State of 
Wyoming, whose address is 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming 82001, hereinafter referred 
to as the “City,” and Cheyenne Housing Authority, whose address is 3314 Sheridan Street, 
Cheyenne, Wyoming 82001, hereinafter referred to as the “Contractor.” 
1. 
SCOPE OF SERVICES:  The Contractor shall, in a satisfactory and proper manner 
as determined by the City, perform the obligations set forth in Appendix A, attached hereto and 
incorporated by this reference, hereinafter referred to as the “Program.” 
 
2. 
TERM:  The Contractor shall commence performance of this Agreement on the 1st 
day of July, 2023 and shall complete performance to the satisfaction of the City no later than the 
30th day of June 2024. 
 
3. 
FEES:  The City agrees to pay Contractor a total amount not to exceed One 
Hundred Thousand Dollars ($100,000.00) for the services set forth in Appendix A upon 
acceptance of performance by the City.  Payment is to be made in one lump sum on or before the 
15th day of December 2023.   
 
Payment by the City to the Contractor for the services performed under the terms and 
conditions of this Agreement is contingent upon the availability of the City funds which are 
appropriated and allocated for payment of such obligation.  The City shall notify the Contractor at 
the earliest possible time of any such shortage or depletion of funds. 
 
4. 
RIGHT TO TERMINATE AGREEMENT:  It is mutually agreed and understood 
that the City reserves the right to terminate this Agreement at any time, provided, however, that 
5:16 pm, Aug 31 2023

 
Page 2 of 10 
the City shall pay the Contractor on a pro rata basis for services rendered up to the time of 
cancellation.  In the event of termination, all property and documents, data, studies and reports 
purchased, prepared or related to this Agreement whether completed or in progress shall 
immediately become the property of the City and shall be delivered to the City within five (5) days 
of termination.  Notwithstanding the above, the Contractor shall not be relieved of liability to the 
City for damages sustained by the City because of any breach of this Agreement by the Contractor, 
and the City may withhold any reimbursement to the Contractor for the purpose of set-off until 
such time as the exact amount of damages due the City from the Contractor is agreed upon or 
otherwise determined. In the event the Contractor fails to strictly perform in accordance with this 
Agreement, the City may elect to correct the deficiencies and charge the Contractor. 
 
5. 
PUBLICATION AND PUBLICITY:  The Contractor may not publish results, data 
or findings arising from its performance of this Agreement in the absence of the prior written 
approval of the Human Services Advisory Council.  A copy of the publication will be furnished to 
the City. 
 
6. 
WYOMING LAWS GOVERN:  The construction, interpretation and enforcement 
of this Agreement shall be governed by the laws of the State of Wyoming; and the courts of the 
First Judicial District, Laramie County, Wyoming shall have jurisdiction over any action arising 
out of this Agreement. 
 
7. 
COVENANT AGAINST CONTINGENT FEES:  The Contractor warrants that no 
person, selling agency or other organization has been employed or retained to solicit or secure this 
Agreement upon agreement or understanding for a commission, percentage, brokerage or 
contingent fee.  For breach or violation of this warrant, the City shall have the right to annul this 
Agreement without liability or, in its discretion, to deduct from the compensation, or otherwise

recover, the full amount of such commission, percentage, brokerage or contingent fee.

INDEMNIFICATION/HOLD HARMLESS:  The Contractor agrees to indemnify, 
hold harmless and defend the City from and against any and all liabilities, claims, penalties, 
forfeitures and suits, and the cost and expenses incident thereto including reasonable attorney’s 
fees, which may hereafter arise as a result of death or bodily injury to any person, destruction or 
damage to any property, contamination of or adverse effects on the environment, or any violation 
of governmental laws, regulations or orders to the extent caused by (1) Contractor’s breach of any 
term or provision of this Agreement, or (2) any negligent or wrongful act, error or omission by 
Contractor, or its employees or subcontractors in the performance of this Agreement.  Contractor 
acknowledges that it may incur a financial obligation to the City pursuant to the terms of this 
paragraph. 
 
9. 
GOVERNMENTAL IMMUNITY:   The City of Cheyenne expressly reserves the 
right to invoke governmental immunity for any claim arising out of this Agreement pursuant to the 
Wyoming Governmental Claims Act, Wyo. Stat. § 1-39-101 et seq. 
 
10. 
EEOC COMPLIANCE:  All parties to this Agreement assure that no person shall 
be excluded from participation in, denied the benefits of, or otherwise discriminated against, in 
connection with the award and performance of this Agreement on the grounds of age, race, color, 
disability, national origin or sex.  The parties further assure that they will include the language of 
this paragraph in all agreements associated or connected in any way with this Agreement and shall 
cause all existing agreements to similarly include this clause therein. 
 
11. 
EMPLOYMENT PRACTICES OF CONTRACTOR:  The Contractor must comply 
in its employment practices with all applicable state and federal laws regulating discrimination in 

 
Page 4 of 10 
employment.  If the Contractor or its employees or subcontractors are determined to be in violation 
of these requirements, this Agreement may be terminated.  The Contractor shall indemnify and 
hold the City of Cheyenne harmless from any and all claims, demands, causes of action, damages, 
injuries and other liabilities which may be asserted against or imposed upon the City of Cheyenne 
as a result of the employment practices of Contractor or its assigns. 
 
12. 
POLITICAL AND RELIGIOUS PROMOTION PROHIBITED:  None of the 
funds, materials, property or services contributed by the City or the Contractor shall be used in the 
performance of this Agreement for the promotion of (i) any partisan political activity, or to further 
the election or defeat of any candidate for public office or (ii) religious worship, instruction or 
proselytization. 
 
13. 
COMPLIANCE WITH APPLICABLE LAW:  The Contractor agrees to comply 
with all applicable local, state and federal laws, ordinances, rules and regulations in the 
performance of this Program.  Failure to do so will give the City the right to immediately terminate 
this Agreement. 
 
14. 
RECORDS AND ACCOUNTS:  The Contractor shall maintain fiscal records and 
accounts as required by Wyoming law and as required by the City to assure proper accounting for 
Program funds.  These records shall be made available for audit purposes to the City and will be 
retained by the Contractor for three years after the expiration of the Agreement.  The Contractor 
shall file with the City Treasurer an audited financial statement as completed each year with a copy 
to the Cheyenne City Council. 
 
15. 
REPORTS AND INSPECTIONS:  The Contractor shall produce financial, program 
progress and other reports as may be requested by the City and will permit on-site inspections upon 
request by the City. 

 
 
Page 5 of 10 
 
16. 
INSURANCE:  The Contractor must provide proof of the following insurance 
coverages: 
 
a. 
Commercial General Liability Insurance.  For claims arising out of bodily 
injury, illness or death, or from damage to or destruction of property of others, including 
loss or use thereof, with minimum limits of $1,000,000.00 per occurrence, $2,000,000.00 
aggregate, for the entire term of this Agreement. 
 
b. 
Business Automobile Insurance.  Including owned, non-owned and hired 
vehicles with minimum limits for bodily injury and property damage of $1,000,000.00 per 
accident for the entire term of this Agreement. 
 
c. 
Workers’ Compensation.  Workers’ compensation coverage shall be in 
effect, as required by Wyoming law, for all employees or agents providing services under 
this Agreement.  Contractor shall provide the City with proof of workers’ compensation or 
employer’s liability insurance coverage. 
 
d. 
Professional Liability Insurance.  The Contractor shall provide proof of 
professional liability insurance or errors and omissions liability insurance in an amount not 
less than $500,000 to protect the City from any and all claims arising from the Contractor’s 
negligence in the performance of duties under this Agreement. The City prefers that this 
liability insurance coverage be provided pursuant to an “occurrence” policy. 
 
 
If this coverage is provided pursuant to a “claims made” policy: 
 
 
i. 
Contractor shall, concurrently with the execution of this 
Agreement, provide the City with a certificate of insurance demonstrating that such 
coverage is or shall be in effect at the time the Contractor begins the provision of 

 
Page 6 of 10 
services under this Agreement; and 
 
 
ii. 
In the event the Contractor’s services extend into a future 
policy period, the Contractor shall, prior to the policy expiration date, provide the 
City with a new certificate of insurance demonstrating that such coverage is or shall 
be in effect during all periods of time that Contractor will provide services under 
this Agreement; and 
 
 
iii. 
Contractor shall maintain said “claims made” coverage for a 
period of five (5) years following the last date that Contractor has provided services 
under this Agreement; and 
 
 
iv. 
In the event the Contractor or the insurer terminates “claims 
made” coverage prior to the expiration of the periods provided in subparagraphs 
(i), (ii), or (iii) of this paragraph, the Contractor shall provide to the City advance 
written notification of the termination of said coverage and shall provide the City 
with an endorsement for an extended reporting period (“tail coverage”) which shall 
be in effect for a period of time not less than five (5) years following the last date 
that Contractor has provided services under this Agreement. 
 
e. 
Additional Insurance Information.  The Contractor shall name the City of 
Cheyenne as an additional insured by endorsement on its insurance policies, with the 
exception of workers’ compensation and professional liability insurance and shall provide

Contractor shall provide the City with certificates of insurance acknowledging the 
above-stated coverages prior to beginning work under this Agreement. 
 
It is understood and agreed that these policies are primary and not contributory.  All

policies required under this Agreement shall be in effect for the duration of the Agreement.  
It shall be an affirmative obligation upon Contractor to immediately notify in writing the 
city risk manager, city clerk, and city attorney of any fact, circumstance, or occurrence that

coverage required by this Agreement, and failure to do so shall be construed to be a breach

In addition, Contractor shall provide the City with copies of insurance policies 
and/or policy endorsements listing the City of Cheyenne as an additional insured.  The

affect the City’s rights or Contractor’s obligation hereunder. 
 
Any insurance company providing coverage under this Agreement shall have a 
minimum A.M. Best rating of A- (excellent). 
 
17. 
INDEPENDENT CONTRACTOR.  The Contractor shall function as an 
independent contractor for the purposes of this Agreement.  The Contractor shall assume sole

18. 
CHANGES, MODIFICATION, REVISIONS AND AMENDMENTS:  This 
Agreement may not be modified, altered or amended except by written instrument, executed and 
signed by all parties to this Agreement. 
 
19. 
TAXES:  The Contractor agrees to pay all valid taxes, excises, license fees, permit 

 
Page 8 of 10 
fees, bills, debts and obligations incurred by in connection with its operations under this 
Agreement. 
20. 
SUCCESSORS AND ASSIGNS:  It is mutually agreed and understood that all of 
the covenants and agreements contained herein shall extend to and be obligatory on the successors 
and assigns of the respective parties. 
 
21. 
ASSIGNMENT:  Neither party shall assign this Agreement without prior written 
consent of both parties.  Any delegation or assignment shall not operate to relieve either party of 
its responsibilities hereunder.  However, nothing in this Agreement shall prohibit the Contractor 
from subcontracting to a qualified subcontractor. 
 
22. 
CONFLICT OF INTEREST:  In entering this Agreement, the Contractor covenants 
that it presently has no interest, and shall not acquire any interest, direct or indirect, financial or 
otherwise, which would conflict in any manner or degree with performance of the services 
hereunder. In addition, the Contractor covenants that in the performance of this contract, no 
subcontractor or person having such an interest shall be employed.  The Contractor certifies that 
no one who has or will have any financial interest under this contract is an officer or employee of 
the City. 
 
23. 
WAIVER:  The waiver by either party of any term, condition or covenant, or breach 
of any term, condition or covenant, shall not constitute a waiver of any other term, condition or

24. 
THIRD PARTY RIGHTS:  The parties do not intend to create in any other 
individual or entity the status of third party beneficiary, and this Agreement shall not be construed

parties to this Agreement.  The parties to this Agreement intend and expressly agree that only the

25. 
DEFAULT:  Each and every term and condition in this Agreement shall be deemed

Page 10 of 10 
APPENDIX  “A” 
 
 
CHEYENNE HOUSING AUTHORITY 
 
 
 
The Contractor shall perform the following: 
 
Provide services as described in the Older Americans Act of 1965, as amended, whose purpose is 
to develop greater capacity and foster development and implementation of comprehensive and 
coordinated systems to serve older individuals in order to secure and maintain maximum 
independence and dignity in a home environment for older individuals capable of self care with 
appropriate supportive services; remove individual and social barriers to economic and personal 
independence for older individuals; provide a continuum of care for vulnerable older individuals; 
and secure the opportunity for older individuals to receive managed in-home and community-
based long-term care services.  The services provided are described below: 
 
1. 
Title III Part B Supportive Services and Senior Centers.  Provides transportation, outreach, 
information and assistance. 
 
2. 
Title III Part C Nutrition Service.  Provide meals to individuals 60 years of age or older. 
 
3. 
Title III Part D Disease Prevention and Health Promotion Services.  Provides information 
to increase public awareness of how healthy lifestyle choices throughout life reduces risk 
of chronic health conditions in later years. 
 
4. 
Title III Part E National Family Caregiver Support Program.  Provides homemaker 
services, visiting and telephone reassurance, chore and maintenance services, in-home 
respite care and adult day care respite, minor modification of homes to facilitate continued 
occupancy by older individuals, personal care and other in-home services.
