COMMERCIAL LEASE  
(6725 Faith Drive, Cheyenne, WY 80029)  
 
This Commercial Lease (the “Lease”) is entered into as of September 7, 2023, by GBP, 
LLC, a Colorado limited liability company, whose address is 2020 Caribou Drive, Suite 101 Fort 
Collins, CO 80525 (“Landlord”); and the City of Cheyenne, whose address is 2101 O’Neil Avenue, 
Municipal Building, Rm 308, Cheyenne, WY 82001 (“Tenant”). In consideration of the mutual 
promises contained in this Lease and for other valuable consideration, the receipt and sufficiency 
of which are acknowledged, landlord leases the Leased Premises described below to Tenant on the 
terms and conditions contained in this Lease.  
 
1. 
Definitions. In this Lease, capitalized words or phrases defined below shall have 
the meanings indicated:  
  
1.1 
Property. “Property” means the commercial office/warehouse (collectively 
referred to as the “Building”), together with all real and personal property located at 6725 
Faith Drive, Cheyenne, Wyoming 80029, including all improvements thereon (including 
the Building and Common Area) and is legally described as follows:  
 
POINTE PLAZA: LOT 1, BLOCK 3, CITY OF CHEYENNE, COUNTY OF 
LARAMIE, STATE OF WYOMING 
 
1.2 
Leased Premises. “Leased Premises” means the entire Property.  
 
1.3 
Lease Term. “Lease Term” means from October 1, 2023 until September 30, 
2024. 
 
1.4 
Lease Year. “Lease Year” shall mean the twelve-month period beginning on 
the Commencement Date. If the Commencement Date is any day other than the first day 
of the month, then the first Lease Year shall include the partial month in which the Monthly 
Base Rent commences and the next consecutive twelve months.  
 
2. 
Term/Option to Extend. 
 
2.1 
The term of this Lease, and the Tenant’s obligation to pay rent under this 
Lease, shall commence at 12:01 a.m. on October 1, 2023 (the “Commencement Date”). 
Tenant shall receive possession of the Leased Premises on the Commencement Date. 
Unless sooner terminated as provided in this Lease, the term shall end at 12:00 Noon on 
September 30, 2024 (the “Initial Termination Date”).  
 
2.2 
Tenant shall have the option to terminate the Lease at any point during the 
lease term by providing Landlord written notice of their intent to terminate the Lease with 
no less than ninety (90) days’ written notice to Landlord (“Termination Option”). 
9:52 am, Sep 21 2023

3. 
Rent. Tenant shall pay the following rent for the Leased Premises:  
  
3.1 
Base Rent. Tenant shall pay the following Base Rent to Landlord, without 
notice and without deduction or setoff, at the address of Landlord as set forth in this Lease 
in advance on the first day of each month during the Lease Term.  If appropriate, the Base 
Rent for the first month of the Lease Term, shall be prorated for said partial month.  
 
Period  
Monthly Base Rent  
October 1, 2023-September 30, 202  
$13,500.00  
 
4. 
Security Deposit. The Parties both acknowledge that the Tenant paid to Landlord 
upon signing of an earlier Lease twenty-seven thousand dollars ($27,000.00) as security for 
Tenant’s payment of rent and performance of the other terms and conditions of this Lease and a 
prior lease. Landlord may deduct from such amount for the reasonable cost of repairs to the Leased 
Premises for which Tenant is responsible if Tenant does not undertake or pay for such repairs as 
permitted herein (ordinary wear and tear excepted), for any rent delinquent under the terms hereof, 
and for any sum advanced by Landlord to cure any default of Tenant hereunder. If deductions 
occur, Landlord must notify Tenant in writing of the itemized deductions with corresponding 
receipts (if applicable) Tenant shall within 15 days after notice from Landlord, redeposit with 
Landlord such amounts so expended so as to maintain the deposit in its original amount. Failure to 
so redeposit shall be deemed a failure to pay rent under the terms hereof. Nothing herein contained 
shall limit the liability of Tenant as to any damage to the Leased Premises, and Tenant shall be 
responsible for the total amount of any damage and/or loss occasioned by actions of Tenant. 
Landlord may deliver the funds deposited hereunder by Tenant to any purchaser of Landlord’s 
interest in the Leased Premises, whereupon Landlord shall be discharged from any further liability 
with respect to such deposit. Landlord shall not be required to pay Tenant any interest on said 
deposit.   
 
5. 
Utilities/Security and Life Safety/Snow Removal/Trash. Tenant shall be solely 
responsible for and promptly pay all charges for heat, water, gas, electric, sewer service, telephone, 
internet, other communications services, trash, backflow/fire sprinklers/fire alarm inspections and 
monthly monitoring (Security and Life Safety), and snow removal, together with any other utility 
service used or consumed on the Leased Premises. Tenant shall arrange with the utility company 
to make direct payment to the utility company for the utilities used on the Leased Premises and to 
have the same billed to Tenant at the address designated by Tenant. In no event shall Landlord be 
liable for any interruption or failure in the supply of any utility service to the Leased Premises.  
  
If any utility company supplying service to the Leased Premises determines that an additional 
service, impact fee, and/or assessment, or any other type of payment or penalty is necessary due to 
Tenant’s use and occupancy of the Building, nature of operation and/or consumption of utilities, 
said expense shall be borne solely by Tenant. Said expense shall be paid promptly, and any repairs 
or modifications requested by the utility company shall be performed by Tenant immediately.  
  

 
Page 4 of 14  
6. 
Use of Leased Premises. Tenant shall use the Leased Premises for general 
gymnastics facility use and for no other purpose whatsoever except with Landlord’s written 
consent. Tenant shall not allow any accumulation of trash or debris on the Leased Premises or on 
any other portion of the Building. All receiving and delivery of goods and merchandise and all 
removal of garbage and refuse shall be made only by way of the rear and/or other service door 
provided therefor. If the Leased Premises has no such door, these matters shall be handled in a 
manner satisfactory to Landlord. No material may be stored outside the Leased Premises, unless 
first approved by Landlord in writing, and then in only such areas as Landlord designates. Tenant 
shall not commit or suffer any waste on the Leased Premises nor shall Tenant permit any nuisance 
to be maintained on the Leased Premises or permit any disorderly conduct or other activity having 
a tendency to annoy or disturb any other occupants of the Building or any adjoining property. 
Tenant agrees not to install any electrical equipment that overloads any electrical paneling, 
circuitry or wiring. Furthermore, Tenant shall not do or permit anything to be done in or about the 
Leased Premises nor bring or keep anything therein which, alone or together with other causes, 
will in any way increase the existing rate of or affect any fire or other insurance upon the Leased 
Premises or Building, or cause, in whole or in part, cancellation of any insurance policy covering 
the Leased Premises or Building.  
 
7. 
Laws and Regulations. Tenant, at its sole cost and expense, shall comply with all 
laws and regulations of any governmental entity, board, commission or agency having jurisdiction 
over the Leased Premises.  
 
8. 
Parking. All vehicles used by Tenant or Tenant’s employees shall be parked only in 
areas designated by Landlord. Tenant shall ensure that Tenant’s employees park only in the areas 
so designated. If Landlord requests, Tenant shall provide Landlord with the license numbers of 
Tenant’s vehicles and the vehicles of Tenant’s employees.  
 
9. 
Taxes.  
  
9.1 
Real Property Taxes and Assessments. Real property taxes and assessment 
shall be paid by Landlord.  
 
9.2 
Personal Property Taxes. If applicable, Tenant shall be responsible for, and 
shall pay promptly when due, any and all taxes and assessments levied or assessed against 
any furniture, fixtures, equipment and other personal property on or in the Leased Premises.  
 
9.3 
Rent Tax. If a special tax, charge or assessment is imposed or levied upon 
the rents paid or payable under this Lease or upon the right of Landlord to receive rents 
(other than to the extent that such rents are included as a part of Landlord’s income for the 
purpose of an income tax), Tenant shall reimburse Landlord for the amount of such tax 
within 15 days after Landlord gives notice of such tax to Tenant.  
 
10. 
Insurance.  
 

 
Page 5 of 14  
10.1 
Landlord’s Insurance. Landlord shall procure and maintain such fire and 
casualty, loss of rents and liability insurance for the Building as it deems proper and 
appropriate from time to time. Such insurance shall not cover any of Tenant’s property, and 
Tenant shall have no interest in any of the proceeds of such insurance. Landlord shall not 
be required to procure any insurance for Tenant’s benefit. The cost of Landlord’s insurance 
shall be paid by Landlord.   
 
10.2 
Tenant’s Insurance. Tenant shall procure, pay for, and maintain 
comprehensive public liability insurance providing coverage from and against any loss or 
damage occasioned by any accident or casualty on, about or adjacent to the Leased 
Premises. Said liability policy shall be written on an “occurrence basis” with limits of not 
less then $3,000,000 bodily injury liability coverage and $1,000,000 property damage 
liability coverage. Tenant shall also procure, pay for and maintain such property insurance 
as Tenant considers necessary covering Tenant’s leasehold improvements, trade fixtures 
and other personal property. Certificates for such insurance shall be delivered to Landlord 
and shall provide that said insurance shall not be changed, modified, reduced or cancelled 
without 30 days’ prior written notice to Landlord. Landlord shall be named as an additional 
insured on all of Tenant’s insurance policies.  
 
10.3 
Mutual Subrogation Waiver and Insurer Requirements. Landlord and Tenant 
grant to each other, on behalf of any insurer providing fire and extended coverage to either 
of them covering the Leased Premises, Building or other improvements thereon or contents 
thereof a waiver of any right of subrogation any such insurer of one party may have against 
the other or as against Landlord or Tenant by virtue of payment of any loss under such 
insurance. Such a waiver shall be effective so long as Landlord and Tenant are empowered 
to grant such waiver under the terms of their respective insurance policy or policies, and 
such waiver shall stand mutually terminated as of the date either Landlord or Tenant gives 
notice to the other that the power to grant such waiver has been so terminated.  
  
11. 
Trash Removal. Tenant shall contract and pay for trash removal service for the 
Leased Premises.   
 
12. 
Maintenance Obligations of Landlord. Except as otherwise provided in this Lease, 
Landlord shall maintain, repair and replace the Property and its systems. The cost of such 
maintenance and repair shall be paid by Landlord unless any maintenance, repair or replacement 
is necessitated or caused, in whole or in part, by the acts or omissions of Tenant in which case such 
cost shall be paid by Tenant. Such responsibility shall supersede any responsibility of Landlord in 
this Lease.  Whether or not any portion of the Property or its systems requires maintenance, repair 
or replacement shall be determined by Landlord in its reasonable discretion. Tenant shall promptly 
notify Landlord if it becomes aware of any condition that it believes requires Landlords attention 
under this Section 13. Landlord shall not be liable to Tenant for any failure to perform any of the 
foregoing maintenance or repair work, there shall be no abatement of rent and no liability of 
Landlord by reason of any injury to or interference with Tenant’s business arising from any 
maintenance or repair work called for under this Lease. Tenant waives the right to make repairs at 
the expense of the Landlord under any law, statute, or ordinance now or hereafter in effect.  

 
Page 6 of 14  
Regardless of anything in the foregoing, Tenant will not be responsible for the cost of repairs 
resulting from normal wear and tear. 
 
13. 
Condition of Leased Premises. Prior to taking possession, Tenant has had the 
opportunity to inspect the Leased Premises.  Tenant accepts the Leased Premises in its current “as 
is” condition as of the Lease Commencement Date.   
 
14. 
Landlord’s Right of Entry and Inspection – Regular, Emergency, Reletting. 
Landlord, as well as Landlord’s agents and employees, may enter the Leased Premises during 
regular business hours (or at any hour if an emergency exists) to examine the Leased Premises or 
to make such repairs, alterations, improvements or additions as Landlord deems necessary. 
Landlord shall be allowed to take all materials into and upon the Leased Premises that may be 
required therefor, without constituting an eviction of Tenant in whole or in part, and the rent 
reserved shall in no way abate while such repairs, alterations, improvements or additions are being 
made, by reason of loss or interruption of business of Tenant or otherwise.  Regardless of the 
foregoing, if Landlord’s use or repair of the Leased Premises continues for more than ten (10) 
consecutive days, causes a significant disruption to the Tenants business operations, and if such 
use or repair arises out of Landlord’s failure to maintain the Leased Premises as required by this 
Lease, negligence or intentional misconduct of Landlord or its agents, Rent shall be reduced pro-
rata based on the portion of the Leased Premises being used or repaired by the Landlord.  Landlord 
may show the Leased Premises to prospective tenants and may place upon the Leased Premises 
the usual notices indicating that the Leased Premises are for lease or sale.  
 
15. 
Alterations and Additions. Unless Landlord’s approval is first secured in writing, 
Tenant shall not install or erect inside partitions, add to existing electric power service, add 
telephone outlets, add light fixtures, install additional heating and/or air conditioning equipment 
or fixtures, or make any other alterations to the interior or exterior of the Leased Premises. Any 
such alterations approved by Landlord shall be made at the sole cost and expense of Tenant. Tenant 
shall promptly pay for all labor and materials involved in such work; shall not allow any 
mechanic’s lien to be placed against the Leased Premises or the Building; and shall indemnify 
Landlord against, and hold Landlord harmless from, all costs and charges for such work. At the 
end of this Lease, all such fixtures, equipment, additions and/or alterations (except movable 
equipment installed by Tenant) shall be and remain the property of Landlord; provided however, 
Landlord shall have the option to require Tenant to remove any or all such fixtures, equipment, 
additions or alterations and restore the Leased Premises to the condition existing immediately prior 
to such change or installation, normal wear and tear excepted, all at Tenant’s cost and expense. All 
such work shall be done in a good and workmanlike manner and shall consist of new materials 
unless Landlord otherwise agrees. Landlord may require adequate security from Tenant assuring 
no mechanic’s liens on account of work done on the Leased Premises by Tenant and may post the 
Leased Premises, or take such other action as is then permitted by law, to protect Landlord and the 
Leased Premises against mechanic’s liens. Landlord may also require adequate security to assure 
Landlord that the Leased Premises will be restored to their original condition upon termination of 
this Lease.  
 

 
Page 7 of 14  
16. 
Sign Approval. Tenant shall not erect, install or place any sign or other advertising 
material on or in the vicinity of the Leased Premises without the prior written approval of Landlord. 
All signs must meet applicable code regulations and be properly permitted. Tenant shall maintain 
all signage or other advertising material which has been approved by Landlord in good condition 
and repair. Upon expiration or sooner termination of this Lease, all signage or other advertising 
material of Tenant must be removed and all damage caused by the removal shall be fully repaired 
at the expense of Tenant.  
 
17. 
Damage or Destruction of Leased Premises. If the Leased Premises or the Building 
are totally destroyed by fire or other casualty or so badly damaged that, in the opinion of Landlord 
it is not feasible to repair or rebuild same, Landlord shall have the right to terminate this Lease 
upon written notice to Tenant. Such notice shall be given within 90 days after such fire or other 
casualty occurs. If the Leased Premises are partially damaged by fire or other casualty, not caused 
by Tenant’s negligence, and are not rendered untenantable thereby (as reasonably determined by 
Landlord), an appropriate reduction of the rent shall be allowed until repairs are substantially 
completed. Landlord shall undertake and complete such repairs with due diligence. If such repairs 
are not completed within 150 days after the fire or other casualty, Tenant may terminate this Lease 
by giving written notice of termination to Landlord within thirty (30) days thereafter.  
 
18. 
Condemnation. If the entire Leased Premises are taken through condemnation 
proceedings or otherwise by any governmental entity or other entity having the power of 
condemnation, this Lease shall terminate as of the date that legal title to the Leased Premises vests 
in the condemning authority, or the date such authority takes possession of the Leased Premises, 
whichever is earlier. If only a portion of the Leased Premises is so taken, Landlord may either 
terminate this Lease as of the date title or possession is transferred as set forth above, whichever is 
earlier, or continue this Lease in effect, in which event the Base Rent shall be equitably adjusted.  
 
In either a total or partial condemnation, Landlord shall have the exclusive right to any 
award made by the condemning authority, and Tenant waives and relinquishes any and all claim 
Tenant may have against such award and all other claims for compensation or damages against 
Landlord arising from such condemnation.  
 
19. 
Assignment or Subletting.  Tenant may not assign this Lease, or sublet the Leased 
Premises or any part thereof, without the written consent of Landlord, which may be given or 
withheld in Landlord’s discretion.  No such assignment or subletting, if approved by Landlord, 
shall relieve Tenant of any of its Lease obligations, and the performance or nonperformance of any 
of the Lease covenants by subtenants shall be considered as the performance or the 
nonperformance by Tenant.  
 
20. 
Quiet Possession. Subject to Tenant’s compliance with the terms of this Lease, 
Tenant shall peaceably and quietly have, hold, occupy and enjoy the Leased Premises during the 
Lease Term.  
  
21. 
Subordination. Tenant agrees that this Lease shall be subordinate to any deed of 
trust, mortgage, or ground lease that may now exist or which may hereafter encumber the Leased 

 
Page 8 of 14  
Premises and to any and all advances to be made thereunder, and all renewals, replacements and 
extensions thereof. Tenant shall execute and deliver whatever instruments may be required for the 
above purposes, and if it fails to do so within 10 days after demand in writing, Tenant makes, 
constitutes and irrevocably appoints Landlord as its attorney-in-fact in its name, place and stead to 
do so. In the event of the sale or assignment of Landlord’s interest in the Leased Premises to the 
beneficiary of a deed of trust or mortgage, or in the event of any proceedings brought for the 
foreclosure of any deed of trust mortgage encumbering the Leased Premises, Tenant shall attorn to 
the purchaser and recognize such purchaser as Landlord under this Lease.  
 
22. 
Easements. Landlord shall have the right to grant any easement on, over, under and 
above the Leased Premises for such purposes as Landlord determines, provided that such 
easements do not materially interfere with Tenant’s use and occupancy of the Leased Premises.  
 
23. 
Tenant’s Hold Harmless and Indemnification Agreement. Tenant shall indemnify 
and hold Landlord harmless from and against any and all claims, liabilities, losses, expenses, costs, 
judgments, and/or demands, including court costs and attorneys’ fees (a) arising from Tenant’s use 
or occupancy of the Property, (b) arising from any act or omission of Tenant, its agents, contractors, 
servants, employees, licensees or invitees in or on the Leased Premises, and (c) arising from any 
Lease breach or default on the part of Tenant. 
 
24. 
Landlord Indemnity.  Landlord shall release, defend, indemnify and hold Tenant, its 
members, officers and employees harmless from and against any and all claims, liabilities, losses, 
expenses, costs, judgments, damages and demands (including court costs, expert fees, attorney 
fees, remediation costs, government fines and penalties) arising out of the grossly negligent or 
intentional misconduct of Landlord.). 
 
25. 
Acts or Omission of Others.  Landlord, its employees and agents, shall not be 
responsible or liable to Tenant or to Tenant’s guests, invitees, employees, agents or any other person 
or entity, for any loss or damage that may be caused by the acts or omissions of others including 
trespassers on or in the Leased Premises, or for any loss or damage caused by or resulting from the 
bursting, stoppage, backing up or leaking of water, gas, electricity or sewers, or caused in any other 
manner.  
  
26. 
Interest on Past Due Obligations. Any amount payable by Tenant to Landlord not 
paid when due shall bear interest at 18% per annum from due date until paid. Payment of such 
interest shall not excuse or cure any default by Tenant under this Lease.  
  
27. 
Late Charge. Tenant shall pay, in addition to any interest on past due obligations, a 
late charge for any payment due to Landlord which is not paid within 10 days after the due date, 
said charge being $50.00 or 5% of said payment, whichever sum is greater.  
  
28. 
Holding Over – Double Last Month’s Rent. If Tenant remains in possession of the 
Leased Premises after the termination of this Lease, whether by expiration of the Lease Term or 
otherwise, without a written agreement as to such possession, Tenant shall be deemed a month-to- 
month tenant. The Base Rent during such holdover tenancy shall be double the Base Rent paid for 

 
Page 9 of 14  
the last month of tenancy under this Lease. No holding over by Tenant shall operate to renew or 
extend this Lease without the written consent of Landlord to such renewal or extension having 
been first obtained. Tenant shall indemnify Landlord against loss or liability resulting from the 
delay by Tenant in surrendering possession of the Leased Premises including, without limitation, 
any claims made with regard to any succeeding occupancy by another tenant.  
  
29. 
Modification or Extensions. No modification or extension of this Lease shall be 
binding upon the parties unless in writing and signed by the parties.  
 
30. 
Status Statement. If Landlord requests, Tenant agrees to execute, acknowledge and 
deliver to Landlord a statement in writing certifying that this Lease is unmodified and in full force 
and effect (or if there have been modifications, that the same are in full force and effect as modified 
and stating the modifications); Tenant has accepted and occupied the Leased Premises; Tenant has 
not paid rent in advance; Tenant is not aware of any prior assignment of this Lease by Landlord; 
Tenant has no offsets against the rent or claims against Landlord; amount of the total monthly rent 
then being paid by Tenant; and, the date to which rent and other charges have been paid.  
 
31. 
Notice. All notices, demands and requests which may be or are required to be given 
by either party to the other shall be in writing. Those given to Tenant shall be properly given if 
personally served on Tenant or an employee of Tenant, posted on the Leased Premises, or mailed 
to Tenant by United States certified mail, return receipt requested, postage prepaid, addressed to 
Tenant at the Leased Premises as follows:  
 
Jason Sanchez, Director, Community Recreation & Events 
City of Cheyenne 
2101 O’Neil Ave., Municipal Bldg., Rm 308 
Cheyenne, WY 82001 
 
or at such other place as Tenant may from time to time designate in a written notice to Landlord. 
Those given to Landlord shall be properly given if personally served on Landlord or if mailed to 
Landlord via United States certified mail, return receipt requested, postage prepaid, addressed to 
Landlord at:  
 
  
  
GBP, LLC   
2020 Caribou Drive, Suite 101   
Fort Collins, CO 80525  
  
or at such other place as Landlord may from time to time designate in a written notice to Tenant. 
Any notice given by personal service shall be effective upon such service or posting. Any notice 
given by mail shall be effective as of the date of mailing.  
 
32. 
No Recording of Lease – Notice to Mortgagee. Landlord and Tenant agree not to 
record this Lease (or a memorandum of this Lease). Tenant consents to Landlord’s assignment of 
rents and of Landlord’s interest in this Lease to a secured lender of Landlord. Tenant further agrees, 
if requested to do so by Landlord, that it will give to said lender a copy of any request for 

 
Page 10 of 14  
performance given by Tenant to Landlord or notice of default given by Tenant to Landlord, and if 
Landlord fails to cure such default, Tenant will give said lender a 60 day period in which to cure 
the same. Said period shall begin with the last day on which Landlord could cure such default 
before Tenant has the right to exercise any remedy by reason of such default. All notices to the 
lender shall be sent by United States certified mail, return receipt requested, postage prepaid.  
 
33. 
Controlling Law – Venue. This Lease shall be interpreted under Wyoming law. Any 
dispute resulting in litigation shall be resolved in court proceedings instituted in Cheyenne 
Wyoming and in no other jurisdiction.  
 
34. 
Landlord Not a Partner With Tenant. Nothing contained in this Lease shall be 
deemed to make Landlord a partner, agent, associate of or in joint venture with Tenant in the 
conduct of Tenant’s business. The relationship between the parties is and shall at all times remain 
solely that of Landlord and Tenant.  
 
35. 
Partial Invalidity. If any term, covenant or condition of this Lease, or the application 
thereof to any person or circumstance, shall be ruled invalid or unenforceable to any extent, the 
remainder of this Lease or the application of such term, covenant or condition to persons and 
circumstances other than those to which it has been held invalid or unenforceable, shall not be 
affected thereby, and each term, covenant and condition of this Lease shall be valid and shall be 
enforced to the fullest extent permitted by law.  
 
36. 
Default. The occurrence of any one or more of the following events shall constitute 
a default and breach of this Lease by Tenant:  
 
36.1 
The vacating or abandonment of the Leased Premises by Tenant.  
 
36.2 
The failure by Tenant to make any payment of rent or any other payment 
required to be made by Tenant hereunder, within three days after the due date.  
 
36.3 
The failure by Tenant to observe or perform any of the covenants, conditions 
or provisions of this Lease to be observed or performed by the Tenant, other than described 
in Section 40.1 or 40.2 above, where such failure shall continue for a period of seven days 
after written notice thereof by Landlord to Tenant; provided, however, that if the nature of 
Tenant’s default is such that more than seven days are reasonably required for its cure, then 
Tenant shall not be deemed to be in default if Tenant commences such cure within said 
seven day period and thereafter diligently prosecutes such cure to completion.  
  
37. 
Default Remedies. In the event of any default or breach by Tenant, Landlord may 
at any time thereafter, with or without notice or demand and without limiting Landlord in the 
exercise of a right or remedy which Landlord may have by reason of such default or breach, elect 
to:  
  
37.1 
Terminate Tenant’s right to possession of the Leased Premises by any lawful 
means, in which case this Lease shall terminate and Tenant shall immediately surrender 

 
Page 11 of 14  
possession of the Leased Premises to Landlord. In such event Landlord shall be entitled to 
recover from Tenant all damages incurred by Landlord by reason of Tenant’s default 
including, but not limited to, the cost of recovering possession actually paid; the worth at 
the time of award by the court having jurisdiction thereof of the amount by which the 
unpaid rent for the balance of the Lease Term after the time of such award exceeds the 
amount of such rental loss for the same period that Tenant proves could be reasonably 
avoided; all costs of re-letting including tenant finish allowances, rent reductions or 
abatements, and all other incentives; that portion of the leasing commission paid by 
Landlord and applicable to the unexpired term of this Lease; and any leasing commissions 
related to reletting the Property. In the event Tenant shall have abandoned the Leased 
Premises, Landlord shall have the option of (i) taking possession of the Leased Premises 
and recovering from Tenant the amount specified in this paragraph, or (ii) proceeding under 
the provisions of the following Section 41.2; or  
  
37.2 
Maintain Tenant’s right to possession, in which case this Lease shall 
continue in effect whether or not Tenant shall have abandoned the Leased Premises. In such 
event Landlord shall be entitled to enforce all of Landlord’s rights and remedies under this 
Lease, including the right to demand the entire amount of rent herein reserved or agreed to 
be paid for the entire term of this Lease as if the whole rent for the entire term were payable 
in advance.  If any of Tenant’s fixtures, furniture, equipment, improvements, additions, 
alterations or other personal property remain on the Leased Premises after termination or 
expiration of this Lease, Landlord may either:  i) deem the Tenant’s property abandoned, 
dispose of Tenant’s property and charge the Tenant for any disposal costs (offset by any 
sales proceeds received by Landlord which shall not obligate Landlord to seek to sell any 
property); or ii) charge Tenant for storage fees equal to Rent for a period of up to six (6) 
months; during such storage period Tenant may remove its property and the storage fees 
shall cease accruing but at the end of six (6) months Tenant’s property shall be deemed 
abandoned and Landlord  may dispose of Tenant’s property and charge the Tenant for any 
disposal costs (offset by any sales proceeds received by Landlord).  
  
In addition to the foregoing remedies, Landlord may pursue any other remedy now or 
hereafter available to Landlord under Wyoming law.  
 
In the event of any default or breach by Landlord, Tenant shall have all rights and remedies 
now or hereafter available to Tenant under Wyoming law; provided, however, in no event shall 
Landlord nor any of its officers, directors, owners or other representatives be liable for any monetary 
damages and Tenant will look solely to the Landlord’s interest in the Leased Premises to satisfy 
such liability.  
 
38. 
Administrative Charges. In the event any check, bank draft or negotiable instrument 
given for any money payment due under this Lease shall be dishonored at any time and from time 
to time, for any reason whatsoever not attributable to Landlord, Landlord shall be entitled, in 
addition to any other remedy that may be available (a) to collect from Tenant an administrative 
charge of $100.00 or three times the face value of the check, bank draft or negotiable instrument, 

 
Page 12 of 14  
whichever is smaller, and (b) at Landlord’s sole option, to require Tenant to make all future rental 
payments by cash or cashier’s check.  
 
39. 
Hazardous Materials and Environmental Considerations.  
  
39.1 
As used in this Section, “Hazardous Materials” shall mean (a) any chemical, 
material, substance or pollutant which poses a hazard to the Leased Premises or the 
Building, or to persons on or about the Leased Premises, or would cause a violation of or 
is regulated by any Hazardous Materials Laws, and (b) any chemical, material or substance 
defined as or included in the definitions of “hazardous substances,” “hazardous wastes,” 
“hazardous materials,” “extremely hazardous waste,” “restricted hazardous waste,” “toxic 
substances,” “regulated substances,” or words of similar import under any applicable 
federal, state or local law or under the regulations adopted or publications promulgated 
pursuant thereto, including, but not limited to, the Comprehensive Environmental 
Response, Compensation and Liability Act of 1980, as amended 42 U.S.C. Section 9601, 
et seq.; the Hazardous Materials Transportation Act, as amended 49 U.S.C. Sec. 1801, et 
seq.; the Resource Conservation and Recovery Act, as amended, 42 U.S.C. Sec. 6901, et 
seq.; the Solid Waste Disposal Act, 42 U.S.C. Section 6991 et seq.; the Federal Water 
Pollution Control Act, as amended, 33 U.S.C. Section 1251, et seq.; and Sections 15-15-
101, et seq., 25-16-101, et seq., 25-7-101, et seq., and 25-8-101 et seq. of the Wyoming 
Revised Statutes. “Hazardous Materials Laws” shall mean any federal, state or local laws, 
ordinances, rules, regulations, or policies (including, but not limited to, those laws specified 
above) relating to the environment, health and safety or the use, handling, transportation, 
production, disposal, discharge or storage of Hazardous Materials, or to industrial hygiene 
or the environmental conditions on, under or about the Leased Premises. Said term shall be 
deemed to include all such laws as are now in effect or as hereafter amended and all other 
such laws as may hereafter be enacted or adopted during the term of this Lease.  
 
39.2 
Tenant covenants and agrees that Tenant and its agents, employees, 
contractors and invitees shall comply with all Hazardous Materials Laws. Without limiting 
the foregoing, Tenant covenants and agrees that it will not use, generate, store or dispose 
of, nor permit the use, generation, storage or disposal of Hazardous Materials on, under or 
about the Leased Premises or the Building nor will it transport or permit the transportation 
of Hazardous Materials to or from the Leased Premises, except in full compliance with any 
applicable Hazardous Materials Laws. Any Hazardous Materials located on the Leased 
Premises shall be handled in an appropriately controlled environment which shall include 
the use of such equipment (at Tenant’s expense) as is necessary to meet or exceed standards 
imposed by any Hazardous Materials Laws and in such a way as not to interfere with any 
other tenant’s use of its premises. Upon breach of any covenant contained herein, Tenant 
shall, at Tenant’s sole expense, cure such breach by taking all action prescribed by any 
applicable Hazardous Materials Laws or by any governmental authority with jurisdiction 
over such matters.  
 
39.3 
Tenant shall inform Landlord at any time of (i) any Hazardous Materials it 
intends to use, generate, handle, store or dispose of, on or about or transport from, the 

 
Page 13 of 14  
Leased Premises and (ii) of Tenant’s discovery of any event or condition which constitutes 
a violation of any applicable Hazardous Materials Laws. Tenant shall provide to Landlord 
copies of all communications to or from any governmental authority or any other party 
relating to Hazardous Materials affecting the Leased Premises.  
 
39.4 
Tenant shall indemnify and hold Landlord harmless from any and all claims, 
judgments, damages, penalties, fines, costs, liabilities, expenses or losses (including., 
without limitation, diminution on value of the Leased Premises, damages for loss or 
restriction on use of all or part of the Leased Premises, sums paid in settlement of claims, 
investigation of site conditions, or any cleanup, removal or restoration work required by 
any federal, state or local governmental agency, attorneys’ fees, consultant fees and expert 
fees) which arise as a result of or in connection with any breach of the foregoing covenants 
or any other violation contained herein.  
 
39.5 
Upon termination of this Lease and/or vacation of the Leased Premises, 
Tenant shall properly remove all Hazardous Materials generated, stored or handled by 
Tenant. Landlord shall grant to Tenant and its agents or contractors such access to the 
Leased Premises as is necessary to accomplish such removal. Any removal after the Lease 
Term shall be considered Tenant holding over and Tenant shall pay double last month’s 
rent.  If a Phase II environmental audit is conducted, at Landlord’s expense, by an 
independent, duly qualified, third party within six (6) months after the effective date of this 
Lease, upon termination of this Lease, Landlord may require Tenant, at Tenant’s expense, 
to complete a Phase II environmental audit by a duly qualified firm.   
 
40. 
Entire Agreement. This Lease sets forth all the promises, agreements, conditions,  
and understandings between Landlord and/or its agents and Tenant relative to the Leased 
Premises, and there are no promises, agreements, conditions, or understandings either oral or 
written, between them other than as set forth in this Lease.  
 
41. 
Waiver. The waiver by Landlord of any term, covenant or condition shall not be 
deemed to be a waiver of such term, covenant or condition, or any subsequent breach of the same 
or any other term, covenant or condition. The subsequent acceptance of rent by Landlord shall not 
be deemed to be a waiver of any preceding breach by Tenant of any term, covenant or condition of 
this Lease, other than the failure of the Tenant to pay the particular rental so accepted, regardless 
of Landlord’s knowledge of such preceding breach at the time of the acceptance of such rent.  
 
42. 
Time of the Essence. Time is of the essence of this Lease.  
 
43. 
Sale of Leased Premises by Landlord. In the event of any sale of the Leased 
Premises, Landlord shall be relieved of all liability under this Lease arising out of any act, 
occurrence or omission occurring after the consummation of such sale; and the purchaser at such 
sale or any subsequent sale of the Leased Premises shall be deemed, without any further agreement 
between the parties or their successors in interest or between the parties and any such purchaser, 
to have assumed and agreed to carry out any and all of the obligations of the Landlord under this 
Lease.  

 
Page 14 of 14  
 
44. 
Survival of Terms. To the extent necessary to give them effect, the terms of this 
Lease shall survive its termination.  
 
45. 
Miscellaneous. All marginal notations and paragraph headings are for purposes of 
reference and shall not affect the true meaning and intent of the Lease Terms. Throughout this 
Lease, wherever the words “Landlord” and “Tenant” are used, they shall include and imply to the 
singular, plural, persons both male and female, and business entities, and in reading this Lease, the 
necessary grammatical changes required to make its provisions mean and apply as aforesaid shall 
be made in the same manner as though originally included in this Lease.  
 
IN WITNESS WHEREOF, the parties hereto have caused this instrument to be executed as 
of the day and year first above written. 
 
GBP, LLC

       Alexis Joens, Authorized Agent

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2023-09-05_TotalExtraction_87870305.pdf
Page 1 of 8 
CITY CONTRACT NO.__________ 
 
USE AGREEMENT 
 
An agreement between the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, a municipal corporation organized 
and existing under the laws of the State of Wyoming, 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming 
82001 (“City”), and Creative Outdoor, a corporation organized and existing under the laws of the 
State of Wyoming, whose principal address 8875 Hidden River Parkway, Suite 300, Tampa, FL  
33637 (“Company”), to grant the Company the non-exclusive use of the public right-of-way for 
the placement of advertising and to grant the Company the exclusive right to construct and install 
benches, shelters, and concrete pads within and along City bus routes, upon which advertising may 
be sold, and establishing a fee for use of the City right-of-way. 
 
Section 1. Definitions. For the purposes of this agreement, the following terms mean: 
 
a. Sidewalk - that portion of a right-of-way, other than the roadway, which is designed or 
intended primarily for the use of pedestrians. 
 
b. Location - a discreet area within a sidewalk or along a public right-of-way which has been 
approved by the City for the placement of a bench or shelter, or both. 
 
c. Orientation - the positioning of a bench or shelter with respect to the nearest sidewalks, 
streets, and rights-of-way. 
 
Section 2.  Permission Granted; Term and Other Requirements. 
 
a. Permission to Install Benches/Shelters.  Subject to the terms of this agreement, the City 
grants the Company exclusive permission to install benches, shelters and pads within and 
along the City’s public rights-of-way, at such locations as designated by the City pursuant 
to subsection c. of this Section.  No bench or shelter will be installed at any location solely 
for the advertisement value of the location. 
 
b. Term of Agreement.  This Agreement is effective when all parties have executed it and 
approvals have been granted by the governing body of the City of Cheyenne and shall 
remain in effect for ten (10) years, unless sooner terminated by as provided in Section 4(h). 
 
c. Location and Removal. The locations of benches, shelters and pads within City rights-of-
way is subject to the approval of the City’s Public Works Director or his or her designee.  In 
the case of federal, state, or county rights-of-way within the City, the Company will also 
be required to obtain necessary approvals from those governmental entities. The City for its 
rights-of- way may direct the removal of any bench, shelter or pad which presents a serious 
hazard to pedestrian or vehicular traffic by serving a written notice on the Company. The 
Company must remove the bench or shelter within twenty-four (24) hours after receipt of 
the written notice. The City may also, for its convenience and in its sole discretion, direct the 
removal of any bench, shelter, or pad, within its rights-of-way, even if a particular bench, 
shelter or pad does not present any immediate hazards, by serving a written notice on the 
Company. The Company shall remove any such bench, shelter or pad within seventy-two 
(72) hours after receipt of the written notice. If the Company fails to remove a bench, 
12:08 pm, Aug 09 2023

Page 2 of 8 
shelter or pad in accordance with the City’s written notice, the City may, in its discretion, 
remove any bench, shelter or pad referred to in the written notice. The reasonable and 
necessary costs of removal shall be the responsibility of the Company. 
 
d. Bench/Shelter Placement and Specifications. 
 
1. Shelters, benches, and pads will be placed at or near designated bus stops, or other 
locations, as specified by the City, along Cheyenne Transit Program bus routes, where 
passengers are routinely picked up or dropped off and where busses can safely stop. 
Only one shelter and one bench will be permitted at any one location. The City may, 
in its sole discretion, direct the removal or relocation of any shelter, bench, or pad, as 
needed to accommodate route changes in the manner provided by subsection c. of this 
Section. The Company shall be responsible for costs, loss of revenue, loss of profit, 
and other expenses and damages that may be incurred by the Company due to relocation 
of bus routes. 
 
2. In addition to the other requirements of this Agreement relating to the location thereof, any 
bench, shelter or pad may be placed within the City’s rights-of-way at bus stops in 
residential areas only upon the written approval of each owner of real property 
immediately adjacent to the location of the bench, shelter or pad and only upon the 
approval of the City Public Works Director or his or her designee. 
 
3. The Company will construct and install all benches and shelters such that no portion of 
any bench or shelter is within four (4) feet of the adjacent curb. The purpose of this 
requirement is to ensure that pedestrians will have use of four (4) feet of unobstructed 
sidewalk width. In the event a bench or shelter cannot be placed on a sidewalk or other 
paved surface, the Company must construct or provide a concrete pad for the bench or 
shelter that extends at least six (6) inches beyond or outside of any vertical plane 
established by any point on the bench or shelter, and provided further that each such 
concrete pad shall be constructed and installed such that no portion of the same is within 
four (4) feet of the adjacent curb. The Company shall retain ownership of any pads 
provided by the Company pursuant to this subsection and shall have the right to remove 
any such pad upon the termination of this Agreement in accordance with its provisions. 
 
4. No bench, shelter or pad shall be placed in such a manner as to interfere with the safe and 
efficient passage of pedestrians, especially persons with disabilities, or to impede 
access to or use of traffic-control devices or other street fixtures. Any bench or shelter 
provided or installed by the Company pursuant to this Agreement shall comply with 
applicable accessibility standards of the Americans with Disabilities Act. 
 
5. It is the sole responsibility of the Company to ensure that all shelters, benches, and pads 
are placed within the public right-of-way and not on private property. The City is not 
responsible for the removal or relocation of any bench, shelter or pad which is located 
on private property, regardless of whether the City has approved any such location. At 
least ten (10) business days prior to the placement of a bench, shelter, or pad the 
Company shall provide written notice of the potential placement of the bench, shelter 
or pad to the owner or owners of the real property which is immediately adjacent to the 
right-of-way where the bench or shelter is to be located and shall obtain all approvals to 
the extent private property is to be used for the location of any bench, shelter or pad. 
 

Page 3 of 8 
6. Each bench must have a maximum height of forty-eight inches (48") and a maximum 
length of eighty-four inches (84"). Each bench must weigh a minimum of three hundred 
pounds (300 lbs.) or be secured in place. No bench or shelter shall be coated or painted 
with fluorescent, day-glow, reflective or other bright or brilliant colors. In the event of 
damage to the City’s real property or improvements associated with the installation of 
a bench, shelter or pad, the Company shall, within seventy-two (72) hours, repair any 
such damage or otherwise return the location to its original condition. 
 
e. Advertising. 
 
1. Except for the use of four (4) shelters previously placed by NextMedia for the display of 
advertising materials pursuant to a separate written agreement between the City and 
NextMedia, the Company has the exclusive right to sell or rent advertising space on the 
backs of benches and shelters which are provided by the Company pursuant to this 
Agreement. The subject matter of the advertising must be suitable for general public 
display and must not include advertising of alcohol or tobacco products; advertising that 
promotes illegal activities or transactions; or advertising that is misleading, deceptive, 
or obscene. The City has the right to direct the removal of advertising materials, words, 
pictures, and other signs which is likely to mislead or distract traffic in accordance with 
subsection d.,3., above. 
 
2. On benches, advertising is limited to the front side of the back rest. 
 
3. All services performed by the Company, including the sale of advertising, shall be in 
the capacity of an independent contractor and not as an officer, agent or employee of the 
City. 
 
f. Maintenance and Repair. 
 
1. The Company agrees to maintain all shelters, benches and advertising materials in 
an attractive, clean and safe condition, and within ten (10) days clean, repaint, replace 
or repair benches, shelters and advertising that have become damaged, weathered, 
defaced, or stolen, regardless of whether any shelter or bench has been negligently or 
intentionally damaged, vandalized or stolen. 
 
2. The City may direct the removal of any bench or shelter that has been vandalized 
with graffiti by serving a written notice on the Company. The Company must repair or 
repaint any such bench or shelter within seventy-two (72) hours after receipt of written 
notice. In the event, the Company fails to repair or repaint any such bench or shelter 
within the time period directed by the City, the City shall have the right to proceed with 
such repainting or repair and the Company shall be responsible for the reasonable and 
necessary costs thereof. 
 
g. Inspections and Reports. 
 
1. The Company must inspect each bench and shelter at least once during each thirty (30) 
day period following the execution of this Agreement and shall correct any and all 
safety deficiencies noted during such inspections. On or before January 1st and July 1st 
of each year following the execution of this Agreement, the Company shall submit an 
itemized list of all locations where any shelter or bench is located. Each such itemized 

Page 4 of 8 
list submitted by the Company shall be compared to a list maintained by the City. The 
City and the Company shall attempt to mutually resolve any differences between any 
list submitted by the Company and any contemporaneous list created by the City, 
provided however, that each party to this Agreement shall be responsible for the costs of 
resolving any discrepancy created by that party’s negligence. 
 
2. In the event the Company fails to conduct any inspection or fails to submit any report 
as required by subsection g., 1., the Company shall, in lieu of or in addition to other 
remedies for breach specified in this Agreement, be responsible for the payment of a 
liquidated penalty of Ten Dollars ($10.00) for each day any such inspection or report is 
late. 
 
h. Public Service Benches.  
 
The Company shall provide public services benches for the promotion of the Cheyenne Transit 
Program and other City projects or events, provided however, that the total number of benches 
provided by the Company shall not exceed seven percent (7%) of the total number of benches 
placed by the Company pursuant to this Agreement. There shall be no charge for the display of 
advertising materials displayed on public service benches, if such advertising is requested by 
the City, except that the City will pay the Company standard advertising production charges. The 
Company shall not be obligated to produce or display advertising on public service benches 
without advance payment of production costs by the City. These public service benches will 
be placed within the right-of-way at locations to be designated by the City for the convenience 
of CTP passengers. 
 
i. Additional Requirements.  The Company agrees to: 
 
1. Within ten (10) days after executing this agreement, the Company will designate a 
person, officer, or other representative to receive official communications from the City 
regarding this Agreement and the Company shall provide the name, business 
address, business telephone number and business e-mail address of the designee. 
 
2. Within thirty (30) days after execution of this Agreement, remove all benches and shelters 
that are not in locations approved by the City. All existing benches and shelters that have 
already been placed as of the date of execution of this Agreement shall be brought into 
compliance with the standards of this Agreement within six (6) months. 
 
3. The Company shall file Certificates of Insurance with the City of Cheyenne verifying 
each type of insurance coverage listed below. The Certificates of Insurance shall be 
submitted to and approved by the City of Cheyenne prior to commencement of 
performance under this Agreement. 
 
a. Type of Coverage

b. It is understood and agreed that these policies are primary and not contributory.  
All policies required under this Agreement shall be in effect for the duration of 
this Agreement.  It shall be an affirmative obligation  
upon the Company to 
immediately notify in writing the city risk manager, city clerk, ad city attorney of 
any fact, circumstance, or occurrence that has resulted in or may result in the 
cancellation or substantive change of any insurance coverage required by this 
Agreement, and failure to do so shall be construed to be a breach of this 
Agreement. 
 
c. The City of Cheyenne shall be named as an additional insured on the 
Company’s insurance policies, except workers' compensation, and the 
Company shall provide a copy of the endorsements providing this coverage. 
 
d. The City has the right to reject a certificate of insurance if any insurer 
selected by the Company is widely regarded in the insurance industry as 
financially unstable. Any insurance company providing coverage under this 
contract shall have a minimum A.M. Best rating of A- (excellent). 
 
e. The City has the right to review the insurance certificates of any or all 
subcontractors who may be engaged by the Company to perform its 
obligations hereunder. Further, the City requires that each subcontractor's 
insurance coverages be at least equivalent to that required of the Company. 
 
f. The City shall have the right to consult with the Company’s insurers and 
insurance agents for disclosure of relevant policy information, but the City’s 
failure to request or review such policies, endorsements, or certificates shall not 
affect the City’s rights or the Company’s obligations hereunder. Disclosure 
of relevant policy information would specifically involve, but is not limited to 
exclusions, deductibles, claims in progress which could significantly reduce the 
annual aggregate limit. 
 
4. Each bench or shelter must have the Company’s name and telephone number legibly placed 
on the front edge of the seat or shelter entrance. All existing benches and shelters shall 
meet this requirement within sixty (60) days after the execution of this Agreement. 
 
Section 3. Use of Sidewalks. The Company will locate, construct and maintain its benches and 
shelters to avoid endangering the lives or property of the City or the general public, interfering with 
City improvements, or hindering or obstructing the use of public sidewalks. Whenever necessary 
to construct or maintain any bench or shelter, or to in any manner disturb or interfere with any 
sidewalk, the Company must, at its own expense, promptly replace and restore the sidewalk to the 
condition in which its existed before work commenced. If any sidewalk becomes uneven, unsettled 
or otherwise requires repair due to Company activities, the Company will, at its own expense, 

Page 6 of 8 
promptly, upon receipt of notice from the City, make any necessary repairs. If the City makes public 
improvements, during this agreement, which alter the grade of any City right-of-way, the Company 
will, upon written notice from the City, at its own expense, promptly remove, or relocate any affected 
bench or shelter and restore all City property to the same condition which existed prior to the 
relocation. The Company agrees that it will obtain a permit from the City for each bench and shelter 
it locates on the City’s right-of-way pursuant to this Agreement. Whenever the Company is working 
on any sidewalk, it will provide adequate warning and safety measures in compliance with federal 
law, the Cheyenne City Code and state statutes. If the Company fails to satisfactorily restore a 
sidewalk, the City may, at its discretion, refuse to issue further permits to the Company. 
 
Section 4. General Provisions. 
 
a. Amendments. No change, modification, revision or amendment to this Agreement shall be 
effective unless the same has been reduced to writing and executed by all parties to this 
Agreement. 
 
b. Applicable Law. The construction, interpretation and enforcement of this agreement will be 
governed by the laws of the State of Wyoming. The courts of the State of Wyoming will 
have jurisdiction over this agreement and the parties, and the venue will be the First Judicial 
District, Court, Laramie County, Wyoming. 
 
c. Compliance with Laws. The Company will keep informed of and comply with all 
applicable federal, state and local laws, rules and regulations in the performance of this 
Agreement. 
 
d. Entirety of Agreement. This agreement consisting of seven (8) pages represents the 
entire and integrated agreement between the parties and supersedes all prior negotiations, 
representations and agreements, whether written or oral. 
 
e. Indemnification; Governmental Immunity. In entering into the Agreement, the 
Company agrees to defend, hold harmless, and indemnify the City of Cheyenne, and its 
officials, employees, agents, and volunteers from and against any and all claims and costs, 
including attorney's fees, arising during or resulting from the Company’s performance of this 
Agreement, and shall carry insurance as set forth in this Agreement. The Company 
acknowledges its understanding of this paragraph and realizes it may have a financial 
responsibility to the City. The City does not waive any applicable defenses and expressly 
reserves the right to invoke governmental immunity pursuant to the Wyoming Governmental 
Claims Act, Wyo. Stat. 1-39-101, et seq., for any claim arising out of performance of this 
agreement. The Company will also compensate the City in full for the loss, damage or 
destruction of the City’s property that in any way arises from or is related to this Agreement 
or activities undertaken by the Company pursuant to this agreement. 
 
f. Notice. All notices arising out of or from the provisions of this Agreement will be in 
writing and given to the parties either by regular mail or personal delivery to the City 
at the address indicated above and to the Company to those persons or officers who have 
been designated to receive such notices pursuant to this Agreement. 
 
g. Termination. In the event of a breach of this Agreement, the City shall serve a written notice 
of breach and a demand for cure upon the Company, and the Company shall then have thirty 

Page 7 of 8 
(30) days to cure its breach. In the event the Company fails to cure its breach within said 
thirty-day period or any extension thereof agreed to by the City, the City shall have the right 
to terminate this Agreement, to sue for specific performance of this Agreement, and to sue 
for damages for breach, as the City may determine in its sole discretion. The Company 
shall remain the owner of each bench, shelter, and pad provided and installed pursuant to 
this Agreement subject to the City’s right to enforce any judgments which may be entered 
in favor of the City for breach of this Agreement. At the expiration of this agreement, or 
upon its earlier termination as provided herein, the Company shall, within thirty (30) days of 
termination, remove all benches, shelters, and pads unless any such bench, shelter or pad 
has been attached for the enforcement of any judgment debt owed to the City. Each bench, 
shelter or pad that is not removed following termination, within the time limit provided 
herein, shall become the property of the City of Cheyenne and may be disposed of, or used 
as the City chooses, without payment or other remuneration. The Company shall repair any 
and all damage to adjacent real property or improvements which may be damaged during the 
process of removal of any bench, shelter or pad. 
 
h. Forbearance. The waiver of any breach of any of the terms or conditions of this 
agreement will be limited to the act or acts constituting such breach and will never be 
construed as being a continuing or permanent waiver of any such terms or conditions, all 
of which are and will remain in full force and effect as to future acts or happenings 
notwithstanding any such individual waiver of any breach thereof. 
 
Section 5.  Construction Standards.  All benches and shelters erected by the Company for the 
purposes of conducting its business shall comply with the Cheyenne City Code and the Unified 
Development Code in effect at the time of the commencement of construction of any such bench or 
shelter. The design and materials of each bench and shelter must be approved by the Transit 
Manager, in coordination with the City’s Traffic Engineer, the City’s Development Office, and such 
other City agencies and departments as required for the issuance of any permit necessary for the 
installation of any such bench or shelter. 
 
Section 6. Compensation. For benches, the Company will pay to the City a sum equal to the greater 
of twenty percent (20%) of annual advertising revenue or One Hundred and Three Dollars 
($103.00) per ad face per year. For shelters, the Company will pay to the City a sum equal to the 
greater of five percent (5%) of annual advertising revenue or Forty-Five Dollars ($45.00) per ad 
face per year. Payment of the compensation specified in this Section shall be paid in twelve (12) 
installments, the payment will be due within 90 days following the completion of the month the 
payment relates to. All funds will be paid to the City Treasurer. An accounting statement 
specifying all shelter and bench locations (and dates of installation) will be provided by the 
Company with each monthly payment. 
 
Section 7. Assignment. The Company may not assign or transfer this agreement without the prior, 
express written permission of the City. 
 
Section 8. Powers; Conflict. The Company acknowledges that its rights are subject to the police 
powers of the City to adopt and enforce general ordinances necessary for the safety and welfare of 
the public. Any conflict between the provisions of this agreement and any other present or future 
lawful exercise of the City’s police power will be resolved in favor of the latter. 
 

Page 8 of 8 
Section 9. Renewal. There is no right or expectation of renewal of this agreement. This agreement 
may not be renewed except upon the written affirmative mutual agreement of the parties. Any 
renewal of this agreement will not be effective until reduced to writing approved by the governing 
body of the City of Cheyenne. 
 
Section 10. Signatures. The parties to this agreement, through their duly authorized 
representatives, have executed this agreement on the days and dates set out below and certify 
that they have read, understood, and agreed to the terms and conditions of this agreement.
